CATCHLINE — CURRENT LOCAL GAME CONTEXT
Prepared by Codex 2 (WSL CLI), 2026-10-17. Read this before older embedded documents.

Snapshot authority:
This captures real local bytes, including uncommitted/untracked PREP035/PACE036. Remote main and proto/deck-sim were observed at9bcc965a06ceb6e135bee46d303e1eec69987523 on this turn. This is not a new GitHub push. Some older docs describe a template-only remote, blockout-only Tier4, inactive prep or obsolete food/storage/fuel rules; those statements are superseded by newer implementation and owner completion records. Code is evidence of implementation; reports describe recorded verification, not new tests by this packaging task. Live play feel and physical touch acceptance remain unverified.

The game:
Unity6000.3.25f1, URP17.3.0. Single-player active fishing/crew automation, harbour purchases/hiring/contracts, chart destinations, voyage fishing and storage, explicit return and sale. Automation frees captain hands for active work. No passive idle income. Runtime world/UI largely assembled by scripts; SampleScene is not the implementation inventory.

Current source map:
GameData.cs: species, map pools, hulls, baseline timings/equipment/economy.
GameState.cs + GameStateProgression.cs + GameStateNetLoadout.cs: cargo/weights, specimen prices, freshness/prepared quality, market/sales, contracts, crew, fuel, save migration, hull/upgrades and net stock.
DeckActors.cs + VoyageGame.cs: rod state machine, casting/strike, crew roles/work, physical carrying and stowing.
VoyageNet.cs + VoyageNetGame.cs + VoyageNetLoadout.cs + VoyageNetUnload.cs: set/soak/haul timing/tension mini-games, shallow/deep yields, mass limits, uses, failures and unloading receipts.
VoyageCompactFisher.cs + VoyagePrepFisher.cs: real boat grid/routes/anchors and hull behaviour.
VoyagePrepOrders.cs: current two-person prep/chum orders, reservations, filters, stationary work phases and controls.
VoyageHud.cs + VoyageInterface*.cs + CoastalCanvas.cs: HUD, crew, shops, goals and native controls.
VoyageDeparture.cs + VoyageTravel.cs + VoyageHaul.cs: departure manifest, travel/fuel, return/cargo sale.
VoyageChart.cs + VoyageIllustratedChart.cs: destinations, chart UI and map selection.
VoyageCatchRewards.cs + CatchPresentation.cs + CatchRewardGraphic.cs: actual existing catch cards, rarity/size styling, records and net receipt.
Assets/Editor/*Checks.cs: executable isolated regression fixtures and assumptions; not all are necessarily current production specifications.

Current implemented facts to orient discussion (verify details in source):
10species: Herring, Mackerel, Pollock, SeaBass, Conger, Plaice, Cod, Whiting, RedMullet, Turbot. Existing IDs/order must survive new content. Rare presentation currently classifies SeaBass/Conger/Turbot, with top-quartile size distinction. Gold/red presentation is reserved for future species, not arbitrary expensive common catches.
Saved hull IDs0rowboat,1skiff,2compact fisher,3prep fisher; naming/tier ladder design may differ from older proposals. Prep fisher6.8x3.5m approved and integrated,4hired berths plus captain,100kg/150kg upgraded trial hold,3rods/1net; final paid unlock not decided. Current compact refit4000coins; skiff refit1500and16fish goal. Current runtime skiff hold18kg base (27upgraded); older documents can disagree. Rowboat20/35kg.
Cargo accepts one whole fish while below capacity, even if that one exceeds it; next delivery is full. Net basket backlog remains real cargo waiting for delivery, not lost fish. Net limit10kg or15kg heavy system; heavy system5000coins. Deep nets100coins/3uses, local larger specimens, one use spent on failure rather than destroying all remaining uses.
Fuel10minutes/20upgraded, burns with voyage time, return at10%, refill1coin/missing minute, no automatic dock refill. Early food inactive; normal crew pace.7 remains. Crew contracts expire after2/3/5voyages; speed1/1.5/2 and base deckhand fee30/60/150; fishermen cost twice. Both roles always offered to fill all hull berths.
Fish sale depends on kg, species-relative size premium1..1.5, market, freshness and preparation. Raw grace60seconds, loss.07/min to.6 unless ice. Prepared freezes current quality permanently, never restores lost freshness, adds one25%premium. Quotes and actual sequential-market sale need comparison, not multiplying all fish by one static market quote.
PREP035: max2ordinary deckhands in Prep, distinct reserved fish remain in hold accounting; maintain10chum including in-flight claims then prepare; priority swap, target10/20/off, small-input/coin filters and preparation minimum controls. One raw input=>one voyage-only chum, consumed before paid bait and expires at return including baited hooks. Heavy local species/top-quarter sizes favoured. Cancelling keeps unfinished fish. No captain cutting mini-game, species picker or drag reorder yet.
PACE036: deckhand pickup/release3xfaster, captain/walking unchanged; prep stays in stable spots through idle/no-input/refill. Base take.15s+cut.9chum/1.1prepare+place.15s before grade/pace. Owner regression actual identical-pace pickup.468s/release1.002s against1.4/3s,2ms sampling. Owner native/screens/build verification is recorded in reports; this packaging run did not repeat it.
Rod play: bait/cast, wait/nibbles, timed strike; reeling is existing rod state work. Do not invent a big-fish tension fight from future design or concept art. Net set game requires timed taps in a moving good zone; haul builds progress/tension via discrete taps, tension decays and progress slips, red zone/mistakes can fail. Exact implementation below. Hold-to-repeat is not net input.

# Catchline

<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->

## Platform

adaptive

Unity native game with a custom illustrated coastal interface, shipped for
Windows and landscape mobile. Native Canvas/input/render checks apply here;
browser styling and Lighthouse are not applicable.

## Product Purpose

An active fishing automation game: start with a small rowboat, catch and carry
fish by hand, sell in Gull Harbour, and gradually build a working boat with
temporary crew and equipment. The hands-on fishing should remain satisfying.

## Capabilities and Constraints

The rowboat/skiff voyage loop already works. Preserve gameplay, save compatibility,
deck footprints, asset pivots and roles. Windows Codex owns runtime implementation;
the existing WSL Codex owns models and reference renders in the art worktree.

## Brand Commitments

Mo confirmed on 2026-10-15: closely match the existing goal renders, with the
fishing view (water, boat, HUD and catch feedback) setting the polish standard.
References: Art/STYLE.md, Art/Concepts/17-hud-fishing.png,
18-hud-harbour.png, 20-title-screen-1280.png and the Day 3/Day 28 references.

## Product Principles

- Finish a small playable slice to a premium visual and interaction standard.
- Visual language is part of the fun, not a cosmetic phase after feature growth.
- Every new feature inherits the finished standard when it is implemented.
- Keep play deliberate and active; automation frees the captain for bigger jobs.
- Use actual game captures and player feedback to judge the result.

## Evidence on Hand

Existing models, rigged crew, Fredoka font, complete supplied UI artwork and water
textures. The first-voyage loop and skiff regressions pass. Mo tested the core and
confirmed it works; the previous presentation lacked the desired polish.

## Open Decisions

Mo chose quiet, tactile coastal sounds on 2026-10-16: sea/harbour ambience,
wood/rope, cast/splash/reel and gentle catch/sale feedback. Minimum mobile
hardware and final mobile accessibility sizing remain unconfirmed. Mo authorizes a bounded fish-content/economy/bait/audio and catch-portrait pass on
2026-10-16, followed by distinct fish model polish. No monetization work.

## Player review — 2026-10-15

POLISH-002 is rejected as a visual target. Passing functional checks does not
mean the presentation is accepted. UI, water and world remain far from Mo's
references. The next pass must make a substantial visual improvement before
content expansion. Mo authorizes reuse from Plunder the Seas and suitable online
assets. Existing WSL artist now owns a complete authored first-location diorama
(ART-011), with Windows owning live water, faithful UI and runtime integration.

## Current Mo content direction — 2026-10-16

Mo asks for more fish in the existing locations, better rewards for big catches,
stronger value from squid, additional bait purchase options, quiet sound effects
and UI polish. Fish must have distinct authored models and matching polished
catch portraits. Keep the current voyage loop and unlimited free scraps. Native
review stays background-only; Windows owns water and scratch integration.

FILE: DESIGN.md

# Catchline visual standard

## Authority and scope

Close translation of the existing goal renders, confirmed by Mo on 2026-10-15.
The fishing view sets the bar; harbour, menu and subsequent features inherit it.
Art/STYLE.md remains authoritative for model geometry, palette and materials.

## World

An inviting miniature working harbour. Broad bevels, honey timber, ivory rails,
navy paint, warm sunlight, cool ambient fill and small busy sailors. No global
outlines, noisy texture detail, heavy bloom, depth of field or glittering ocean.
Frame the boat generously, leaving quiet water for interface and interactions.

## Tokens

| Role | Value |
|---|---|
| Ink / navy | #173F57 |
| Cream surface | #F4EFE3 |
| Primary action | #E36A35 |
| Sea / secondary action | #168F9F |
| Water highlight | #64C7C4 |
| Bite / attention | #E6AD38 |
| Success | #3E9A5B, accompanied by words or an icon |
| Error | #C8443A, accompanied by recovery copy |
| Typography | supplied Fredoka Medium; navy on cream, ivory on navy |

## Composition and components

- Fishing: compact cream status panel upper left; coins/pause/return upper
  right; portrait crew strips lower left; action bubbles attached to the task.
  The centre belongs to the boat. Strike panel occupies lower-centre water.
- Use supplied sliced artwork, icons and fish/crew portraits consistently.
  Icons get real space; do not pad button strings with spaces to fake alignment.
- Stable readable type hierarchy, generous internal padding, short copy and
  grouped resources. Avoid rows of tiny debug statistics.
- Catch reward: restrained portrait, species, weight, value and record badge.
  Never cover the Carry action or open a blocking reward modal.
- Menu: supplied illustrated harbour, real logo and left-aligned cream menu
  with one clear primary action. Settings and pause share the component family.
- Harbour shops use the same typography, illustrated headers, item icons,
  accessible controls and explicit owned/unavailable states.

## Motion

Calm layered sea colour and broad moving light patterns. Narrow broken hull
contact foam; wakes only while travelling. No dense white bars crossing the sea.
Feedback enters briefly and settles; animations must not delay actions.

Harbour departure first slides sideways clear of the quay, then turns onto the
selected fishing ground's bearing. Return reverses the journey, aligns parallel
and slides into berth. Turn about the hull centre; the camera keeps the whole
manoeuvre readable. Water highlights stay restrained enough to show shallow
seabed and underwater kelp/fish; dense white sheen is rejected.

Sound is quiet tactile coastal foley, confirmed by Mo on 2026-10-16. The captain's
cast, splash, bite and reel are audible; crew automation stays quiet. Keep the
sea bed subdued, avoid melodic arcade rewards, and expose a sound control.

## Acceptance

Compare actual native captures to the references at desktop and landscape-phone
sizes. Verify waiting/bite/reel/catch/carry/full/return states, menus, affordability
and save behaviour. Preserve deliberate changes in shared components; fix those
components rather than independently reskinning each feature later. Native Unity
render/input checks replace browser/Lighthouse checks for this project.

## Revised world requirement — POLISH-003

Author the complete camera-visible fishing-location patch as a cohesive miniature
coastal scene: connected shoreline and cliff forms, grass/vegetation, deliberate
working-coast detail and submerged shelf/seabed. Derive dimensions from gameplay
camera coverage, with framing margin. Mo's 10x20 m example expresses bounded
scope, not literal dimensions. Generic scattered pale rocks are rejected.

Use detailed Blender-authored static environment geometry with live Unity water,
boat, shadows and interactions. Water must support animated normals/waves,
shore/hull contact, shallow/deep variation and per-location colour controls.
Evaluate Plunder's adapted Boat Attack package before inventing another minimal
water shader. A beauty render is a composition target; runtime parity must be
checked independently. The UI still requires a faithful rebuild against goal
renders, especially References/day03-small-boat.png; POLISH-002 is not accepted.

### Water direction — Mo, 16 October 2026
Clean stylized blue 3D water with broad rounded wave shapes visible from the
fishing camera. Use the current PTS clean preset as the reference. Reduce fine
surface noise; keep white glints sparse and foam/wash disabled. Codex 1 owns the
water adaptation; Codex 2 owns the new skiff work and its separate design brief.
Keep all verification hidden while Mo is at school. Current water is a working
iteration, not visually accepted.

### Reference update — WATER-006 (supersedes WATER-005 wave scale)
Mo supplied two third-party game screenshots on16 October. Target: clear stylized
water with small overlapping ripple/reflection texture and transparent shallows.
Colour may differ. Large geometric swells are rejected while the boat remains
steady; retain only tiny surface displacement. Zero foam/white wash stays. Keep
this direction over the preceding request for pronounced rolling wave shapes.

Water motion — WATER-007: ripples run at65% of WATER-006 speed. Gentle hull
heave/pitch/roll follows hull-averaged Gerstner heights on the same clock, capped
at.8degrees tilt. Preserve a steady readable deck and route heading/centre; pause
freezes the shared motion. Foam stays disabled.

## Cohesive native interface — HUD-008
Fishing uses one connected cream/navy instrument, portrait crew rail, world
interaction bubbles and a single illustrated fishing-action instrument. Keep
hold count/value, food/bait and contract progress readable without covering the
boat. Catch feedback shares portrait, typography and button materials.

Harbour is a proper shop workspace: smaller harbour/departure view, illustrated
category rail and shop sign, roomy two-column product displays, prices and
explicit disabled/owned/prerequisite states. All five categories inherit the
same layout; retain real inventory, contracts, sale and upgrade rules.

Use the supplied Fredoka and86-texture kit consistently. ScreenSpaceOverlay
keeps UI artwork clear of world tonemapping. Respect safe areas, logical phone
targets, Larger type, visible keyboard selection and existing confirmations.
Native capture evidence/checks and remaining physical input limits are in
`docs/HUD-008.md`; browser/Lighthouse checks do not substitute for Unity evidence.

## Direct reference HUD — UI-024

Mo requests literal reproduction of Downloads/fish.png crew/right and day/time/coin/goal
left, superseding HUD008 lower-left crew placement for the fishing view. Use the
shared FishReferenceStyle/CoastalCanvas reference primitives and measured1672x941
coordinates. Reuse original cream contours/icons/static headings; no independent
fonts or restyling per side. Actual crew/data stays honest; no fake professions,
levels or percentages. Phone readability and final pixel parity remain unaccepted.
See docs/CLI-REFERENCE-HUD-024.md for delivery/native evidence and sampled static pixel comparison; dynamic font identity is unproven, so do not call it pixel-perfect.

## Illustrated coastal map — CHART017, 2026-10-16

Mo's map reference replaces the former paper/vector chart. Use the richly
illustrated full-screen coastal plate at Resources/Charts/gull-coast: crystalline
turquoise shallows, dark-blue channel, kelp, sandbar, rocky headlands and a warm
working harbour. Keep the supplied geography and visual richness. Overlay real
native pins, white dashed routes, cream cards, navy reference lettering and the
Boat/Crew/Map/Gear/Port navigation. Prices, selection, money, hold and contracts
are live state; never bake them into artwork. Landmark coordinates belong to the
illustration; they do not claim compass-exact cartography. Contract/details panels
open on demand so the map remains the main screen. Keep44physical-pixel targets.
Reference and final native views: docs/screens/chart017. Image generation prompt
and integration/verification handoff: docs/CHART017.md.

## Departure manifest and fuel — FUEL021

Set sail opens a focused cream coastal departure sheet using the existing sliced
reference panels, Fredoka, supplied supply icons and exact crew portraits. Destination
and wallet lead; fuel/bait purchases sit beside real inventory, crew/contract duration
and fitted net remain a read-only manifest. Back to port and Let's go are clear final
actions. Desktop and landscape phone inherit the native safe-area logical canvas.
Food indicators are replaced by fuel percentage; oar-powered boats explicitly need
no fuel. No hunger/penalty language or crew/upgrade selling belongs in this sheet.

# Catchline — art style and modelling contract

Status: concept and first-prop handoff for `art/style-pass`. The PNG sheets are modelling references. Actual Blender meshes and FBX exports now accompany them: the [six Crab Coast props](Models/Props/REPORT.md) and [26 Gull Harbour assets](Models/GullHarbour/REPORT.md). Unity was not opened for either pass. The gameplay prototype and its scenes/scripts are outside this branch's changes.

## Visual authority

Mo's [Day 3 small-boat](Concepts/References/day03-small-boat.png) and [Day 28 tuna-boat](Concepts/References/day28-tuna-boat.png) mockups are the visual targets. Preserve their inviting miniature working-boat character: navy hulls, warm timber, ivory rails, orange safety equipment, small busy sailors, sea teal and soft sunlight. Aim for their shape language and material warmth as closely as possible. The PNGs in `Concepts/` adapt that look into isolated orthographic references for modelling; they are not screenshots of a finished Unity build.

Use chunky silhouettes, broad bevels, a few deliberate planar facets and clean colour blocks. Low-poly does not mean every surface is triangulated visibly. Round heads, gloves and boots can use smooth normals; hull corners, tools and machinery retain selected flat planes. Keep most detail in silhouettes and large parts rather than texture noise, tiny rivets, scratches or complicated piping.

**Outline decision: no global outlines.** Separate shapes using value, colour, bevel highlights and restrained contact shading. Do not add black inverted-hull or screen-space outlines to every object. A selection ring or local interaction highlight is a separate gameplay affordance.

## Palette

These are proposed canonical **sRGB base colours**, chosen to match the mockups' visual families. They are not pixel samples from lit renders. Exact values here override generated colour-chip text and image sampling. Lighting will change their apparent colour.

| Use | Hex | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Deck wood | `#C58B50` | Warm honey timber; broad planks, sparse seams |
| Wood highlight | `#DFB77A` | Restrained lighter plank variation |
| Wood recess | `#8C5A36` | Seams and sheltered underside only |
| Hull navy | `#173F57` | Hull, frames, captain garments |
| Hull lit blue | `#2A6685` | Optional secondary painted panel |
| Rail white / ivory | `#F3F0DC` | Rails, cabin, crate rims; avoid pure white |
| Safety orange-red | `#E36A35` | Equipment, beanie, buoy, lifering, trim |
| Sea teal | `#168F9F` | Sea, sorter bib and trays |
| Shallow-water highlight | `#64C7C4` | Broad moving accents, not glitter |
| Yellow oilskins | `#E6AD38` | Deckhand and engineer helmet |
| Steel | `#8FA6AC` | Sorting surface, simple metal details |
| Rubber / deepest shadow | `#28343B` | Boots, roller, rope recesses |
| Rope | `#D1AF76` | Warm rope; `M_Rope` |
| Band ivory | `#F3DEA1` | Pale yellow crab safety bands; `M_BandIvory` |
| Kelp green | `#477A56` | Kelp and pollock back; `M_KelpGreen` |
| Fish silver | `#BAC9C6` | Fish flank; `M_FishSilver` |
| Skin base | `#B9784B` | Initial shared reference; later variants reuse body |
| Sheet / UI cream | `#F4EFE3` | Reference background and future panel family |

Reserve orange for useful accents rather than painting every station orange. Role readability also comes from hat silhouette. Status indicators need icons/shape as well as colour; hat colours alone do not communicate station state.

## Materials and light

- Timber: matte, wide planks, subtle broad value variation, little grain. Planks follow the boat's fore-aft axis. Do not model every seam as a gap.
- Painted hull and equipment: clean opaque painted surfaces with small bevels. The navy hull should still read blue in shadow. Keep metallic response low on paint.
- Steel worktops: muted satin rather than mirrored chrome. Rubber is dark matte. Glass uses dark blue opaque/low-complexity glazing for early models; avoid needing cabin interiors.
- Nets and pot mesh: sparse coarse openings, thick readable outer frames. Treat tiny knots as texture or omit them; do not create thousands of strands for a concept-matching first asset.
- Water: teal base with restrained broad highlights, gentle motion and a narrow foam/contact treatment around the hull. Avoid physically simulated waves or dense reflections as prerequisites for this style.

Lighting target: soft warm sunlight from the upper-left of the gameplay frame, cooler ambient fill, short soft shadows, lightly darkened contacts. Maintain readable equipment in shadow. Avoid crushed blacks, strong bloom, depth of field, film grain, sharp specular glitter and excessive ambient occlusion. Begin with restrained neutral colour grading; saturated palette colours do the work.

**Moving-boat caveat:** a boat that changes layout or moves through world space must not rely on a world-fixed scene lightmap for its changing shadows. Achieve the baked-light *look* with controlled materials, optional asset-local baked AO/vertex shading, ambient fill and a small real-time lighting setup. Evaluate actual scene baking only for genuinely fixed geometry. Moving crew need a compatible dynamic lighting path. Do not stack strong baked AO with strong screen-space AO.

URP implementation is a later integration step, not part of these concepts. [Unity 6.3 URP Baked Lit reference](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.3/Documentation/Manual/urp/baked-lit-shader.html) and [Unity light probes](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.3/Documentation/Manual/LightProbes.html) describe available lighting mechanisms; the aesthetic recommendations above are this project's art direction, not a claim that lightmaps can follow arbitrary changing layouts.

## Camera and reference conventions

- Game: landscape, fixed orthographic 3/4 camera. Starting art-review angle: about 45 degrees down from horizontal, yaw about 35 degrees off the stern axis; tune later for readable jobs and clear walkways.
- Model sheets: true orthographic front/right/back/top, plus an orthographic 3/4 where helpful. A 3/4 view is still parallel-projected, never a perspective hero shot.
- Rendered hull top plans and starboard side elevations: bow to the right, stern to the left. In those top plans port is up and starboard down. The exact grid supplement deliberately uses bow-up for coordinate readability and labels its orientation. Hull plan and elevation must agree about cabin, motor and rails.
- Station front: the operator-facing edge. For the hauler, the plan labels its sea edge on the right and worker edge below; mount that sea edge against the rail. Rotation on deck is allowed; dimensions rotate with it.
- Neutral background and soft contact shadow are presentation only; do not bake either into albedo.

Generated multiview sheets can contain small view-to-view differences or misleading ruler/grid marks. **Written dimensions, the exact grid supplement and a single coherent model take precedence.** Treat views as shape/colour references and reconcile them in Blender; they are not CAD or guaranteed multiview reconstruction inputs. For image-to-3D, isolate one subject/view rather than feeding an entire labelled sheet as a single object. Inspect resulting topology, scale and hidden surfaces manually.

## World scale and axes

- One deck tile = **1 m × 1 m**. Model in metres, apply transforms before export, use positive unit scale.
- Unity convention: `+Y` up, `+Z` toward bow, `+X` toward starboard. Blender modelling is Z-up; verify export conversion so the imported Unity result follows this convention.
- Hull pivot: centre of its rectangular build deck at deck height, `(0, 0, 0)` in local Unity coordinates. Deck walking plane is `Y = 0`; hull extends below it.
- Station pivot: footprint centre at deck contact, `Y = 0`. Front working edge faces local `-Z`. Define working/interaction points separately from visual meshes.
- Crew root: floor between boots, facing local `+Z`. Hand attachment points are on the shared rig. Crate pivot: centre of underside. Crab pivot: ground centre. Buoy pivot: centre of base. Pot pivot: centre of underside. Prop roots use their horizontal bounding-box centre with minimum height at zero; variant parts share the same root origin.
- Footprint borders, dimension arrows, labels and scale figures are guides, never mesh geometry.

## Crew: one shared body

Body height is **0.95 m sole to bare scalp**. Hats add approximately 0.06–0.10 m above the scalp (module origins overlap the head); do not stretch different roles to fit their hats. Proposed shoulder width 0.34 m, head width about 0.30 m, head height about 0.33 m. Short thick legs, rounded mitten gloves with a thumb, chunky rubber boots, a small nose and very simple eyes. Keep fingers, ears and facial features economical. Initial proportions are art targets, not anatomy measurements.

All five jobs share body geometry, proportions, skeleton, hand positions and animation set. Hats are removable modules; role clothing primarily uses recoloured material regions on the shared garment silhouette. Initial sheets use the same skin/hair to make this relationship obvious. Later skin/hair variants do not require a new body or job rig.

| Role | Hat silhouette | Main clothing | Readable cue |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Captain | White peaked cap, navy band | Navy with ivory trim | Wide white cap |
| Deckhand | Orange rolled beanie | Yellow oilskin bib, navy sleeves | Orange + yellow |
| Sorter | Navy baseball cap | Teal bib, navy sleeves | Forward cap peak + teal |
| Bander | Short-brim orange-red cap | Orange-red bib, navy sleeves, ivory gloves | Red-orange workwear |
| Engineer | Yellow hard hat | Muted steel-grey bib, navy sleeves | Helmet dome + brim |

The six reference actions are **walk, carry crate, work at table, idle, cheer and panic**. Use one animation library for all roles. For carry, two hands support a 0.45 × 0.32 × 0.25 m crate near waist height; use an attachment constraint/pose rather than letting the crate intersect the belly. For table work, the 0.55 m worktop is deliberately low enough for a 0.95 m sailor. Cheer and panic must remain distinct at gameplay zoom. Pose sheet drawings are key-pose intent, not animation timing or a final rig.

## Hulls

Hull 1's 3 × 2 tile deck and hull 2's 5 × 3 tile deck are confirmed by Mo. Their outer hull dimensions remain proposed art envelopes; hull 3's deck and centred wheelhouse footprints are authoritative. Keep hull topology, rail thickness, timber treatment and navy/ivory/orange material family consistent across tiers.

| Hull | Overall hull length × beam | Rectangular build deck, fore-aft × across | Fixed content |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 — Rowboat | 4.2 × 2.4 m | 3 × 2 tiles | Removable rowing bench, separate oars; no motor/cabin |
| 2 — Outboard skiff | 6.5 × 3.5 m, excluding motor | 5 × 3 tiles | One-tile forward console, outboard outside stern |
| 3 — Day boat | 12.0 × 5.8 m | **10 × 5 tiles** | **3 across × 2 fore-aft tile wheelhouse, centred at bow**, low white rails |

Day boat: deck rectangle includes the wheelhouse. It has 50 tiles total, of which 6 are cabin, leaving 44 before equipment and aisle allocations. Do not interpret the requirement as a 10 × 5 aft work area plus another cabin area. The tapered bow and hull margins are outside the build rectangle.

Exact day-boat deck in local Unity coordinates: `X = [-2.5, +2.5]`, `Z = [-5, +5]`, `Y = 0`. Tile columns `c = 0..4` run port to starboard; rows `r = 0..9` run stern to bow. Tile centre is `(c - 2.0, 0, r - 4.5)`. The wheelhouse is **centred**, occupying **columns 1, 2 and 3, rows 8 and 9**: **3 m across × 2 m fore-aft**, `X = [-1.5, +1.5]`, `Z = [+3, +5]`. One clear tile column remains on each side. Its centre is `(0, 0, 4)` on the deck plane.

Proposed day-boat outer hull extends to `X = ±2.9`, `Z = [-5.5, +6.5]`. Keep the full build rectangle inside the hull; start the bow taper ahead of `Z = +5`. Wheelhouse body height: 1.65 m above deck, with roof, fittings and small mast above. Rail height: about 0.60 m. These chibi-scale proportions prioritise a visible deck and matching the references over real vessel certification.

Keep cabins, rail sections, motor, oars, hatch covers and fittings modular. Empty hull references intentionally omit stations so equipment arrangement stays a gameplay choice. Avoid decorative props blocking walkways or hiding job animations.

## Location order

**Location 1 is Gull Harbour**, beginning with manual rod fishing on the 3 × 2 m rowboat deck, then progressing towards nets. Harbour Mouth supplies herring/mackerel, Kelpbank pollock/sea bass, and Rocky Point the rare conger eel. Cooler storage, bait and voyage provisions support the active gameplay loop. **Crab Coast is location 2.** The older crab sheets remain valid asset references, but do not describe the starting location. This follows the live `proto/deck-sim` checkout’s `docs/GAMEPLAY.md`, read before this modelling pass; that working document was not yet committed when read.

## Gull Harbour tier 1 — built asset contract

Sources, exports, measured bounds and topology checks are in [the Gull Harbour report](Models/GullHarbour/REPORT.md). Source Blender axes are X right, -Y forward, Z up; exported Unity axes are X right, +Z forward, +Y up. Part origins listed below are **Unity local metres**, relative to the asset root. Rotations are zero and scales one; offset part translations deliberately locate animation pivots. Moving parts are siblings under one empty root. No rig or animation clips are included.

| Asset | Size / root | Moving parts and placement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Rowboat | Hull 4.2 m fore-aft × 2.4 m beam; deck 3 × 2 m, deck-centre root at Y=0 | Plank deck, centre thwart, separate oars. Oar pivots at (-1.12,.49,0) and (1.12,.49,0). Port sockets (-1.115,.44,-1) and (-1.115,.44,+1): 0.5 m and 2.5 m from **rectangular deck stern** Z=-1.5. Bow taper lies outside the build rectangle. |
| Fishing rod | Butt root; 1.6 m upright from butt to tip | Separate reel handle, pivot (.107,.24,0). Rotate/attach whole rod for casting. |
| Bobber | 0.12 m total height, 0.08 m diameter; waterline root | Lower 0.045 m submerged, upper 0.075 m above water; orange-red/ivory bands. |
| Cooler / ice box | 0.80 × 0.50 × 0.45 m W × D × H; underside root | Identical body mesh; lid origin (0,.40,-.25), rear hinge, rotate local X. Ice variant has teal lid and solid ivory frost motifs. |
| Bait bucket | 0.30 m diameter; approximately 0.367 m including bail | Underside root; open interior. Art-selected size. |
| Lunch tin | 0.25 × 0.18 × 0.12 m body; latch adds 0.004 m depth | Underside root; separate lid. Art-selected size. |
| Herring / mackerel / pollock / sea bass / conger | Exact nose-to-tail lengths 0.26 / 0.34 / 0.48 / 0.50 / 0.80 m | Face +Z, grounded root; separate tail at body-tail join. Mackerel stripes are geometry, no textures. |
| Shared crew | 0.95 m sole-to-scalp; T-pose, ground root between boots | One connected closed all-quad body with separate face/garment details. No armature. Hat anchor (0,.91,0); cap, beanie and hood roots at their mounting seats. |
| Quay | 4 m long × 2 m deep; walking surface/root Y=0 | Piles extend below walking plane; end-to-end repeat along X. |
| Harbour dressing | Bollard, 2.8 m lamp, two houses, 6 m lighthouse, three rocks | Grounded roots. House walls 3×2.6 m and 3.4×2.8 m; roof overhangs included in reported bounds. Art-selected dimensions. |
| Kelp | Exactly flat at Y=0, floating root | Drift-aligned overlapping leaf silhouettes and four inset bulb disks; a planar union prevents overlapping faces. Use a two-sided material for underwater views. |

Palette material names extend the prop batch with `M_DeckWood`, `M_WoodHighlight`, `M_WoodRecess`, `M_HullBlue`, `M_OilskinYellow`, `M_Skin`, `M_KelpGreen` and `M_FishSilver`. All use the canonical sRGB colours above. No bitmap textures or Unity `.mat` assets are included. Preview lights/background are source-only and excluded from FBX.

## Crab Coast stations

Footprint is reserved occupancy, not a demand that every mesh touches its tile border. Listed visual envelopes leave inset margins. Work positions and routes live in adjacent walkable tiles and are not included in the station occupancy. Do not place another machine over those access positions.

| Station | Footprint | Visual envelope W × D × H | Design and operation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pot hauler | **1 × 1** | 0.85 × 0.85 × 1.25 m | Navy pedestal, compact orange davit, rubber roller; back at rail, worker inboard |
| Sorting table | **2 × 1** | 1.80 × 0.80 × 0.55 m | Satin steel top, two teal trays, navy legs; front edge along long side |
| Banding bench | **1 × 1** | 0.80 × 0.80 × 0.55 m worktop; tool to 0.85 m | Wood top, ivory restraint pad, orange applicator, teal band spool |
| Live tank | **2 × 1** | 1.80 × 0.80 × 0.65 m | Navy tub, ivory rim, teal water, two compartments; aerator inside envelope |
| Pot rack | **2 × 1** | 1.80 × 0.80 × 1.10 m | Navy open frame, timber shelves; four pot slots, two wide × two high |

Pot hauler's rigid structure stays inside its tile. Rope and a suspended moving pot may travel outboard into water during the hauling animation; neither reserves an extra deck equipment tile. Avoid an oversized crane boom that visually sweeps through neighbouring stations. Station plan drawings must not imply additional pipework, handles or stored pots projecting into an aisle.

One-metre clear aisles are the layout target. Keep operator-facing edges accessible; two-tile machines can rotate by 90 degrees. Animated parts get separate meshes/pivots: hauler roller, banding applicator, tank surface, pot rack contents. Do not join crabs, water, rope and machine into one uneditable object.

## Crab Coast props

| Prop | Proposed envelope | Shared parts / treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Empty pot | 0.70 × 0.55 × 0.35 m | Chunky 0.036 m navy rails/posts, coarse mesh, four closed rope lashings, broad teal hatch latch |
| Full pot | Same as empty | Same pot mesh, toggle a small crab load inside |
| Crab | 0.28 m width across legs, 0.18 m body length | Orange-red, two broad claws, four short walking legs per side |
| Banded crab | Same as crab | Same crab mesh plus ivory-yellow band on each closed pincer |
| Crate | 0.45 × 0.32 × 0.25 m | Navy slotted box, ivory chunky rim, integrated handles |
| Buoy | 0.25 m diameter × 0.38 m height | Orange-red, broad ivory stripe, small rope eye |

The full pot is a state of the empty pot, and banded crab a state of the crab. Match their silhouettes and transforms. The prop sheet is the canonical prop design: reuse that pot and crab mesh in station content slots rather than modelling each small station thumbnail as a different species or trap. Keep the crate small enough to carry with the shared rig; do not use a 1 m cube for a 0.95 m worker. Optional pots/crabs in station views are content examples, not permanently fused station geometry.

## Build 1 UI kit

The [UI handoff](../Assets/Art/UI/UI.md) documents 44 transparent PNG sprites, their logical/export sizes and slice borders, plus Fredoka Medium and its OFL licence. Cream panels, navy text, orange primary, teal secondary and oilskin-gold bite feedback extend the established visual world. Selected cards and action bubbles deliberately use local borders as interaction affordances; world art still has no outlines. Green `#3E9A5B` and red `#C8443A` are the existing prototype's progress/status colours, always accompanied by words or icons.

[16 — kit sheet](Concepts/16-ui-kit.png), [17 — fishing HUD](Concepts/17-hud-fishing.png) and [18 — harbour HUD](Concepts/18-hud-harbour.png) are exact 1920×1080 compositions built from the exported kit, with Blender-rendered project assets behind the two HUDs. They are design mockups, not Unity captures. Reproducible vector geometry, compositors, stage scripts and validation live in `Art/Source/`. The existing crew asset is unchanged; the mockup stage temporarily lowers the copied crew's arms in memory and never saves it back to the model source. Tier 3 skiff UI remains the next task.

## Modelling acceptance checks

1. Compare silhouettes and colours to the Day 3/28 references; maintain softness without turning objects into glossy toys or flat icons.
2. Check body height, deck grid and station bounds numerically in Blender. Written values and the exact grid supplement override generated dimension marks.
3. Keep role body/rig shared, hats separate, pot/crab state variants reusable, and animated station components separate.
4. Check models in front, side, top and the intended orthographic gameplay camera. Keep feet, hands, crates and worktops from intersecting.
5. Check at landscape phone gameplay size: hats, station purpose, carrying state and walkways must read. These concepts do not establish a device-performance benchmark.
6. During later Unity integration, verify scale, shading, shadows and visibility on the actual prototype and target hardware. No editor or runtime validation is claimed by this art pass.

Plaice lies flat with both eyes on top; Cod retains a separate tail and a short chin barbel. Perched Seagull is 0.4 m nose-to-tail with separate wing pivots.

ShopFront_FishMarket, ShopFront_HarbourMaster, ShopFront_CrewOffice, ShopFront_Chandlery and ShopFront_Supplies have ground roots at the door centre, front facing Unity −Z; place the root at the building door rather than centring the decoration bounds. They leave a central approach clear. MarketStallAwning is a separate open stall. SandBank is exactly flat at waterline, 4 × 2 m; CliffSegment8m repeats along X with matching ends and a level green top. Two-sided material handling is required for views below flat sea dressing.

## Task 04 skiff kit

Skiff keeps hull 2’s 5 × 3 m build deck centred at Y=0, with bow +Z and a 6.5 × 3.5 m hull envelope excluding the motor. Task 04 supersedes the forward-console proposal: the low console occupies stern-starboard tile c=2, r=0 (zero-based columns port→starboard, rows stern→bow), Unity centre (1,0,−2). Three port rod sockets sit at Unity Z=−1.75, 0, +1.75. Propeller spins around local Z. The open hold is built into the deck; HoldLid rotates about local X, from its hinge at Unity (0,.025,.5).

HandWinch reserves 1 × 1 m with a separate Crank. SortTable reserves 2 × 1 m; its .55 m worktop, tray, chute and open bin stay within that footprint. GillNetCoil sits on deck; GillNetFloatLine and GillNetSection repeat every 2 m along local X. The float-line root is at waterline, and laid mesh lies .18 m below it. The kit has coarse visible geometry and no simulation or animation clips.

# PACE036 - quicker fish handling, stationary prep crew

Mo's correction supersedes PREP035's per-job station allocation and idle behaviour.

- All deckhands now pick up and release fish at **3x** the previous action speed. Applies to rod catches on every hull and gathering/stowing net catch. Walking speed and captain timings are unchanged. On the Compact/Prep fisher, base handling durations fall from1.4s pickup/3s stow to approximately0.467s/1s, before the usual grade and crew pace multiplier.
- Prep duty assigns one stable standing position per hand (max2). A hand goes to the table once, including when it has no input yet, and waits there. No cosmetic patrol when stock is full, inputs run out or nothing meets the filter. Another hand leaving does not reassign the remaining worker's position.
- Subsequent jobs do not enqueue walking. Reach for the front input, cut on the table, then turn/reach to the box behind. Fish visual moves; feet stay planted. Base cycle:0.15s take +0.9s chum cut or1.1s preparation cut +0.15s place. The existing grade/crew work multiplier applies.
- Chum-to10 priorities, two-worker reservations, input filters, frozen prepared quality/no spoilage, sale premium, weight accounting and safe cancellation remain intact. A hand already carrying a fish when reassigned finishes that delivery before taking its table position.

Verification and delivery evidence will be appended after the isolated checks and native run. No model/UI redesign or real-save change.

## Verification

`docs/tools/check.ps1 -Name pace036 -Method Catchline.EditorTools.HandlingPaceChecks.ValidateAndBuild` exit0: PACE036, PREP035, TIER033 and COMPACT FISHER checks passed; standalone player built. Actual grade1/pace1 elapsed pickup0.468s, release1.002s (2ms sampling), matching base1.4/3 and3xhandling. Station regression checks both workers' planar positions on every tick across8s empty wait,24fish processed to10chum+14prepared, each take/cut/place phase, immediate refill after chum consumption, and remaining worker keeping its spot when the other changes duty. Complete existing freshness/quality/save/target/cancellation, deck/rod/socket/routes/net/dock and prior-hull checks also passed. The existing compact120s trace logged23landed/21held/0lost; this is a regression observation, not a controlled performance comparison.

Native player `pace036-native.log` confirms TEST/no-save mode and normal capture completion. Inspected the new1440x900 and844x390 working-table views plus844order modal: both hands on separate work positions, fish on boards, cutting activity labels, contained/readable HUD. # PREP035 - Tier 4 working prep table

The approved Prep fisher has a real two-person deckhand station. This continues TIER033; the hull/art dimensions are unchanged.

## Play

Open **Catchline - Tier 4 Test** on the desktop after the updated build is installed. The isolated trial starts with two fishermen, one delivery hand and one Prep hand. Open **Prep table / chum** at bottom left to change orders and assign a second hand. **Crew duties** also offers Fish, Nets and Prep. No more than two deckhands can be assigned to Prep. Crew remain ordinary temporary contracts.

- Default priority: maintain 10 chum, then prepare eligible raw fish. Reserved/in-progress chum counts toward the target. When a hook uses chum, the order becomes eligible again. With no eligible small fish, the hand falls back to preparation.
- Priority swap changes which eligible order is chosen next; jobs already started finish. The first pass uses a swap control, not drag-and-drop.
- Target cycles 10 / 20 / off. Chum input weight cycles 1 / 2 / 0.5 kg; a 10-coin maximum input value also applies. One eligible whole raw fish yields one chum.
- Preparation minimum cycles all / 1 kg / 3 kg. Species filtering and arbitrary numeric text entry are deferred.
- Chum is earned at the table, cannot be bought or saved, and expires on returning to port, including chum left on ready hooks. It is consumed before squid/worms and strongly favours heavier local species and specimens in the top quarter of their species weight range. It does not import species from another map.
- Preparation keeps the same fish object, weight, species and contract eligibility; it grants one 25% market premium. It freezes current quality permanently. It does not restore quality already lost. Prepared fish never spoils, even without the IceBox upgrade. Front raw buckets continue normal freshness loss; ice coolers protect raw inputs too. Prepared boxes need no upgrade.
- Fish remain in cargo while reserved so two jobs cannot manufacture spare hold capacity. Each slot claims a distinct fish. Changing duty, cancellation and returning release claims without losing unfinished inputs. Work continues at full cargo capacity and uses normal crew pace and voyage fuel.

The captain cutting minigame, species filters, drag reordering and a final paid Tier 4 progression unlock are not in this pass. Tier 4 remains the save-isolated trial. With two hands assigned to Prep, arrange someone to deliver rod catches or carry them yourself.

## Implementation

`VoyagePrepOrders.cs` owns orders, reservations, station actions, actual table fish visuals and native UI. `CaughtFish.Prepared` and `PreparedQuality` survive port copy and JSON save/load. Saved `PrepOrderSettings` have legacy defaults; `Chum` is voyage-only. Baited rods remember chum separately from squid, and chum fish rolls stay in the current map pool. Existing work animation is reused for table actions.

## Verification

First isolated run: `docs/tools/check.ps1 -Name prep035 -Method Catchline.EditorTools.PrepOrdersChecks.Run` exit0; PREP035, TIER033 and COMPACT FISHER checks passed. Exact target with concurrent workers, eligibility/fallback/refill, cancellation, third worker rejection, no duplicate premium, spoilage/ice, port/save preservation, voyage-only stock, native UI callbacks and actual quote/payout covered. Source checks are not physical touch acceptance.

Final suite/build and native capture evidence will be appended after completion.

Final behaviour/build: `docs/tools/check.ps1 -Name prep035-final -Method Catchline.EditorTools.PrepOrdersChecks.ValidateAndBuild` exit0. PREP035, TIER033 and COMPACT FISHER suites passed; successful standalone build. Added regression proves already-deteriorated fish keeps its frozen quality after preparation. Native desktop first pass inspected: both station occupants and table fish visible, readable 2/2 work/order/stock controls. First landscape working capture retained the previous modal for one frame; capture hook now waits a frame after each modal state change and also captures Crew duties. Behaviour source unchanged by this capture-only fix; rebuild/recapture pending.

Final capture-only rebuild `prep035-capture` exit0. Final rendered Windows player `Logs/prep035-native-final.log` confirms TEST/no-save mode and PREP035 NATIVE CAPTURES COMPLETE, no native exceptions. Inspected all six final PNGs in `docs/screens/prep035`: working table, order modal and duties modal at1440x900 and844x390; both real station workers and fish visible, all controls contained/readable. Scripted native captures verify presentation, not physical touch acceptance. Captain mini-game/drag/species filters remain explicitly deferred.

Updated `Catchline-play/Tier033` from the verified build. Desktop **Catchline - Tier 4 Test** still targets that executable with `-prepboat-trial -native-display`; executable, game assembly and resources hashes match scratch build (`docs/screens/prep035/installed-build.json`). No real launcher/save changes and no capture process left running. Source UTF8/hash receipt: `verified-source.json`. Temporary PREP035 source backup successfully removed after verification; older task backup leftovers untouched. No commit or push.

# Tier 4 boat integration — TIER033 (2026-10-17)

Mo approved the larger BOAT032 boat exactly. The production asset preserves its
6.8 m hull length, 3.5 m beam, short squared cream canopy with teal brow/beacon,
1.48 × 0.64 m two-person table, knives/boards/chum pail, front raw storage and
rear prepared-fish boxes. The source comparison scene remains unchanged.

## Delivered model

- Editable production scene: `Art/Models/Tier4Production033/PrepFisherV1.blend`.
- Game asset: `Assets/Art/Models/Boats/PrepFisherV1.fbx` and `.placements.json`.
- Repeatable exporter: `Art/Source/export_tier4_033.py`.
- No comparison crew, labels, lights or stage are exported. The game uses actual
  animated crew at their existing scale, not frozen scale-study figures.
- `RawBuckets` and `RawIceCoolers` are independently switchable subtrees.
  `PreparedHold` is always present. Ice Box ownership selects the front variant.
- Two `PrepWorker_*`, two `RawInput_*` and two `PreparedOutput_*` anchors retain
  the approved positions. Rotating machinery and rod sockets have local pivots.
- Native mesh access is enabled so physical routing works in standalone players.

## First game integration

The boat is appended as saved hull ID3; IDs0–2 are unchanged. Four hired berths
plus the captain match the approved five-person layout. Three rods and one
stern net remain. The initial hold stays100kg/150kg with Big Cooler; this is
inherited test tuning, not a final tier4 economy decision. No extra rods/net,
purchase price or unlock goal has been invented.

The game derives walkable space from actual active model geometry at body
clearance heights. Both table positions, every rod, all resting positions,
front delivery access and stern net access connect through the real side aisles.
Changing front storage at port rebuilds the grid for its different footprint.
Net catch lands in a relocated basket behind the prepared boxes. Camera/water
contact dimensions and harbour offset account for the wider hull. Tier3 mass
market/depletion/upgrade rules also apply to tier4.

Select the new trial in the save-isolated Chandlery, or run the isolated player
with `-prepboat-trial`. `-prepboat-ice` selects upgraded front coolers. The trial
starts at Harbour mouth with two temporary deckhands and two temporary fishers,
60worms,200coins and a full tank. Trial-only downgrade to tier3 releases the
fourth trial berth. Real progression still stops at the existing tier3 refit.

## Verification

`docs/tools/check.ps1 -Name tier033-final -Method Catchline.EditorTools.PrepFisherChecks.ValidateAndBuild`
runs against Catchline-sim, never the main project in batchmode. Checks cover

Native Windows player captures include the actual HUD at1440×900 and844×390,
for both storage variants, under `docs/screens/tier033/`. This checks rendered
layout; scripted actions and desktop phone-sized frames do not prove physical
touch acceptance or player approval. A startup-black first capture was found
and the capture warm-up corrected before final delivery.

The editable model, source and normal game references are in the main checkout.
Playable preview build is `Catchline-sim/Builds/Tier033/Catchline.exe`; the
separate installation/shortcut, if present, points to `Catchline-play/Tier033`.
The real player save and existing Real/Tier3 shortcuts are untouched.

## Next station implementation — authorized direction, not present yet

The model and interaction positions are integrated; processing/chum jobs are
not functional in this first boat pass. Keep these requirements when adding them:

1. Up to two ordinary deckhands share one order list and reserve distinct fish
   and worker positions. The captain can help with a quick head-cut green-zone
   minigame. Work should be quick enough to run alongside fishing. Fuel bounds
   optional waiting; the player may wait or go home.
2. Drag to prioritize orders. Maintain10chum is a stock target; when satisfied
   or missing eligible input, lower-priority preparation can run. Using chum
   makes its order eligible again. Finish the current fish before switching.
3. Chum consumes eligible fish under configurable value/weight limits. It is
   earned each voyage, unpurchasable, attracts the largest fish and does not
   carry into the next voyage. Exact yield/time are still tuning decisions.
4. Sale preparation supports species/minimum-weight selection; +25% is a
   provisional bonus, not yet an economy value. Preserve species, specimen
   weight, contracts and cargo mass, and prevent preparing the same fish twice.
5. Raw fish in front buckets loses freshness until treated. The front Ice Box
   upgrade protects raw fish too. **Prepared fish never spoils, even without
   that upgrade**, and goes only then into the boxes behind each hand. This
   requires persistent per-fish prepared state; the current global ice rule
   alone is not the finished processing system.

No commit was created. Existing unrelated dirty work is preserved.

Final delivery verification: tier033-final behaviour suite and tier3 regression
passed; tier033-delivery build-only check exited0 after punctuation cleanup.
All four final native captures were inspected, including the corrected desktop
bucket frame. The installed EXE, game assembly and resource asset hashes match
the verified build. Desktop shortcut `Catchline - Tier 4 Test` targets the new
save-isolated installation with `-prepboat-trial -native-display`.

# Catchline: gameplay design

Single source of truth for how the game plays. Numbers are starting values for tuning, not promises.

**Build priority, confirmed by Mo 2026-10-15:** the core loop works. Finish the
small playable slice's visual and interaction quality before expanding systems.
Closely match the goal renders, with fishing as the reference standard. Every
later mechanic ships using that finished visual language from its first pass.
See PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md and docs/plans/PLAYABILITY-PLAN.md.

## Current tier3/net progression — NET023,16October2026

Tier3 Compact fisher now has100kg hold,150kg with the existing big-cooler upgrade.
Both nets have a10kg hard capacity; Heavy net system costs5000coins and raises both
to15kg. Normal nets favour many small fish; deep nets fewer larger local specimens.
Tier3 upgrade prices scale to1500–3500coins for ordinary equipment. Depletion is
weight-based and market saturation softer on tier3. Real progression can buy the
Compact fisher from an owned skiff for4000coins. See [NET023.md](NET023.md) for
pricing, measured native distributions, save compatibility and launcher separation.
This supersedes WEIGHT020's24/36kg hold and unequal net budgets. Starter prices
and saved owned upgrades are preserved.

## Current crew rules — CREW026, 2026-10-16

Crew remain temporary contracts: hands last2 voyages, experienced crew3 and
veterans5, then leave. The player pays the existing grade/role fee each time they
hire. Every hull's Crew Office keeps enough candidates of both roles to fill all
its berths, even after hiring one; board rolls, hull changes and old-save loads
also restore those choices. Players can fill all three tier3 berths with
fishermen, deckhands or any mix in both Real and Test.

The Test shortcut starts at a tier3 harbour with no hired crew and1000 isolated
coins, opening Crew Office so any three can be chosen. `-deep-net-trial` remains a
separate opt-in at-sea test. Real continues to load and save the player's progress.
Full crew does not remove the captain's interactive work. The empty-roster Test
setup lets players hire their own mix using normal contract fees. See
[CREW026.md](CREW026.md).

## Current fuel/preparation rules — FUEL021,16October2026

Food is inactive on early boats. Crew works at the former70%pace as its normal
baseline. Powered boats carry10minutes of fuel,20with the extra tank (the old lunch
upgrade ID). Fuel burns with voyage time, costs1coin/missing minute to refill and
forces a return at10%. Docks do not refill it automatically; rowboats use oars.
Set sail opens a polished departure manifest with quick bait/fuel purchases before
explicit Let's go. See [FUEL021.md](FUEL021.md). These rules supersede older food and
round-trip fuel descriptions below. A later cook/food bonus remains future work.

## Approved weight-capacity direction — 2026-10-16

Implemented in WEIGHT020 after Mo's approval. Current capacities, pricing, net
uses, migration and evidence are in [WEIGHT020.md](WEIGHT020.md). Earlier numbers
elsewhere in this document are historical starting targets where they conflict.

- Boat storage and its capacity upgrades use kilograms instead of fish slots.
  Ten kilograms was an example, not a required starting capacity. Tune capacities
  to voyage length and crew throughput rather than relabelling existing counts.
- Large specimens should earn more for the same total weight than small fish.
  WEIGHT020 implements a capped species-relative premium from1x to1.5x; current
  rates are first-pass tuning for playtesting, not permanent balance promises.
- If stored weight is below capacity, accept one more whole fish regardless of
  its weight. That fish may exceed capacity, then storage is full. Apply this
  per fish, including concurrent crew deliveries and net unloading, never to an
  entire net batch. Do not silently delete remaining basket fish.
- Balance net yields primarily around catch weight, while preserving individual
  fish, local species pools and specimen records. Deep nets are costly limited-use
  equipment; reserve deep nets remain part of preparation.
- A failed deep-net haul loses that haul's catch and consumes one use, preserving
  all remaining uses. It does not destroy the entire remaining deep net. When its
  last use is consumed, another net is needed. WEIGHT020 starts at100coins/3uses,
  30ssoak and roughly twice shallow target mass, with larger local specimens.
  Worker recovery is80%safe; failure spends one use and loses that haul only.

Saved fish weights and purchased upgrades are preserved; storage HUD/shop wording,
fullness, sale preview/payout and simultaneous deliveries are verified in isolated
native checks. Conditions, rank progression and journal expansion remain
design proposals; these decisions do not approve every pasted expansion detail.

## Current boat progression steering — 2026-10-16

Mo's latest direction uses zero-based numbering: **Boat 0** is a solo tutorial
boat; **Boat 1** introduces hired crew, probably one or two; **Boat 2** has three
hired crew, with net fishing conditional on compelling later boat additions.
The captain is additional to hired crew. Exact Boat 1 capacity and later mechanics
are not finalized. Flexible role composition and uncovered-work warnings remain
the prior intent. CREW026 guarantees role availability without changing the
existing crew cap, fees or expiring contracts; future boat-cap changes still
follow this direction. The
big-fish-fight/fish-finder proposal was rejected.

This is production direction; its boat ladder remains unfinalized. CREW026 keeps
role choices guaranteed without changing those hull-capacity decisions or the
existing temporary contracts.
See [CLI-SKIFF-DIRECTION-020.md](CLI-SKIFF-DIRECTION-020.md). ART-020 authoring remains
paused after baseline inspection; no revised Skiff model has been delivered.

## Current urgent playtest slice — 2026-10-15

Mo's 2026-10-16 direction supersedes the one-candidate-per-role testing override:
enough role contracts to fill the boat now apply to both Real and Test. Contract
durations and expiry remain unchanged. See
[PLAYTEST-OVERRIDES.md](PLAYTEST-OVERRIDES.md) and [CREW026.md](CREW026.md).

Free unlimited scraps automatically replace exhausted paid bait and catch only
small herring/mackerel. Returning to port preserves/saves the haul. The captain
presses **Sell haul +N coins**, with a preview matching market impact and contract
rewards, before the next departure. Haul value and oldest-fish freshness are shown
while fishing: 60 seconds at full value, then 7% loss/min down to 60%, unless iced.
Harbour storage holds value until sale. With a deckhand the captain stays in
fishing mode after a catch and can **Throw again** or **Leave**.

## Pillars

1. **Automation is the game, fishing is the accent.** You start doing every job by hand and slowly build a boat that runs itself, but it never becomes cosy.
2. **Slow, not frantic.** Think Factorio and Satisfactory, not Overcooked. You are rate-limited: by hand you are at best as fast as yourself. Mistakes cost time (a fish gets off, bait is lost), never a game over.
3. **Your hands are the scarcest resource.** Every automation frees your hands from one job and the game hands them a new, bigger one. Events and crew gaps keep pulling you back to the real fishing.
4. **People management never stops.** Crew contracts expire and need replacing,
   but both worker roles stay available to hire. Crew assignment and the captain's
   own active tasks keep the player engaged without random role scarcity.
5. **Fully active.** No idle income. A daily login reward is fine.

## The voyage loop

1. **Harbour:** sell the catch, take contracts, hire crew, buy provisions and bait, buy upgrades.
2. **Map:** pick a fishing spot. Spots differ in species, distance and how depleted they are.
3. **Sail out:** a short animation. The boat view stays fixed; only the surroundings change (harbour quay, open sea, kelp, rocks, deep water, later islands and tropical shallows).
4. **Fish:** until the storage is full, provisions run out or you choose to go home. There is **no voyage timer**: storage size and provisions decide how long you stay.
5. **Sail home** and repeat.

**The voyage is the unit of time** for crew and harbour contracts, spot recovery and market recovery. Day and night can be purely visual inside a voyage.

Target voyage lengths:

| Boat | Cheap crew | Experienced crew |
|---|---|---|
| Rowboat (tier 1-2) | 4-7 min | 3-5 min |
| Skiff (tier 3-4) | 10-15 min | 7-10 min |
| Day boat (tier 5+) | about 20 min | 10-15 min |

Small boats go home often, big boats stay out longer.

## Tier ladder (location 1: the fish harbour)

| Tier | Boat | Runs by itself | What your hands do |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Rowboat | nothing | One rod: bait, cast, watch the bobber, strike on time, pick the fish up, carry it to the cooler |
| 2 | Rowboat | Deckhand baits hooks and carries fish to the cooler | Run 2 rods (rod holder). You are still the only one who strikes on time |
| 3 | Skiff | Fisherman works the rods (worse than a perfect you) | First small net: set it and haul it with a hand winch |
| 4 | Skiff | Net winch; deckhands pick fish out of the net | Sort table (species and size, undersize and bycatch go back), ice the hold |
| 5 | Day boat | Sorting chute, conveyors | Layout and repairs; fish finder to chase schools |
| 6+ | Bigger | Gutting/fillet station, freezer hold, trawl | Processing chains and contracts |

Every tier creates its own bottleneck. Example: the tier 3 fisherman lands fish faster than one deckhand can carry them, so fish pile up on deck until you hire or automate.

### What keeps you close to the fishing

- **Captain's work remains active.** Crew automate their assigned jobs, but a full
  roster does not remove the captain's interactive responsibilities.
- **Sickness and injury.** Seasickness in storms, a hook or pinch injury mid-work, a hangover after a port night. A crew member is out for part of a voyage and you cover.
- **Things break, and you are always needed.** A maintenance crew member fixes things alone, slowly; with you helping it goes fast.
  - Electrical fault: the deck goes dark and the machines stop. Fuse and wiring minigames in the engine room.
  - Leak: pump the bilge while the mechanic patches.
  - Net snagged on a rock: cut it (lose the net) or dive to free it.
  - Rope in the propeller: dive.
  - Storm: secure the cargo, or a rogue wave takes crates.
  - Engine fire: extinguisher minigame.
  - Ice on deck (North Pole): chip it off.

  An event never ends a run; it costs time.
- **Expeditions: the captain fishes for real.**
  - Dinghy runs: the big boat drops you in a rowboat, because shy exotic fish avoid engines. Tier 1 rod play with endgame gear.
  - Big game chair: marlin, tuna, shark. A long tension fight, and the base for the weigh-off.
  - Scuba: spearfishing, lobsters by hand, pearls, wreck salvage.
  - Submarine (late): deep-sea species.
  - Night fishing for squid under lights.
  - One legend fish per location: a multi-step hunt with clues, bait and weather.

## Crew

- **Temporary contracts.** Pay the listed fee at the Crew Office; crew leave after their grade-based contract ends. Both roles remain available, and each hire is replaced by a new candidate.
- **Three grades:**

| Grade | Speed | Contract | Hire fee | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hand | 1.0x | 2 voyages | base | Usually on the board |
| Experienced | 1.5x, +1 trait | 3 voyages | 2x base | Often on the board |
| Veteran | 2.0x, +2 traits, also raises catch quality | 5 voyages | 5x base | Rare |

Fishermen cost twice the same-grade deckhand fee. The board always offers enough
of both roles to fill every berth on the current hull, including tier3's three
fishermen, three deckhands or any mix. Hiring a candidate removes that individual
and replenishes the role; candidate names/grades can change, role availability
does not. This applies in both Real and Test.

- **Hands must feel fine, not bad.** No penalty systems that only hit cheap crew. Every gap between grades is a bonus, never a punishment.
- **Prestige:** crew carry-over between locations is not decided yet; contracts still expire within a location.

### Why experienced crew are worth it (cargo-capped voyages)

Since there is no idle income, **coins per real minute** still matters. Experienced
crew cost twice a hand's fee for1.5x speed and3 voyages; veterans cost5x for2x
speed,5 voyages, extra traits and higher catch quality. The premium is paid per
contract, so it only pays where the worker is a bottleneck.

## Provisions, fuel and the cook

- **Food** drains per crew member per minute. Out of food: everyone works at 70% until home. It never stops the boat.
- **Fuel** drains while sailing (distance to the spot and back). Rowboats are rowed: no fuel, but rowing takes time.
- **Cook** (from the day boat): a normal cook has no downside. A good cook uses less food and gives a morale bonus (+10% work speed). Food poisoning comes only from the cheapest provisions or a rare event, never from a normal cook.
- **Comfort:** bunks, galley and heating raise morale, which raises productivity on long voyages.

## Fishing spots, market and contracts

- **Spots deplete:** every catch lowers a spot's richness (bites come slower). Every voyage, all spots recover a bit. Seasons and migration change a spot's species over time.
- **The market reacts:** each fish sold lowers that species' price a little, and prices recover over a few voyages. Dumping one species is punished; variety pays.
- **Harbour contracts:** a board with 3 offers ("Deliver 8 mackerel within 2 voyages, reward 90"). Take up to 2. A missed deadline means the contract is gone and a new one appears. Contracts are the main pull toward specific spots and species.
- **Rivals:** AI captains on the harbour scoreboard. They also take contracts from the board, so waiting can cost you the good one. A weekly weigh-off ranks you against them.
- **Fish log:** an encyclopedia of every species, size record and location. Collection drives exploration.

## What money buys

- **Coins (earned by playing):** ship upgrades that add **features**, never a bare stat bump. Examples:
  - a cooler, then an ice box (fish keep full value)
  - storage size
  - a rod holder (second rod)
  - net winch speed
  - a fuel-efficient engine
  - bunks (comfort)
  - a galley
  - a fish finder

  Coins also pay crew contracts, provisions, bait and hull licences.
- **Gems (premium, also earned slowly):** cards and chests for **gear**: rods, lines, bait types, nets and tools, with small percentage improvements and visual variety. Also cosmetics, ship pets (dog: crew level faster; cat: scares off thieving gulls and gives +10% income), a season pass and remove-ads.
- **Never sold for premium currency:** crew contracts or hull progress.

## Hull progress

You never buy a ship. Each hull has a **refit licence**: a skill or throughput goal plus a fee. Example rowboat → skiff: fill the big cooler (16 fish) in one voyage, then pay 1,500. The boat is refitted into the next hull model. Run 1 caps at hull 3.

## Locations and prestige

1. **Location 1: fish harbour** (rod → nets, tiers by species: herring, mackerel, pollock, sea bass, cod...).
2. **Location 2: Crab Coast** (pots, tiers by crab species).
3. Later: Tropical Islands, Miami, North Pole.

Unlocking a location's whole map enables prestige: sell the company and start at the next location. Each reset costs more and adds a tier. Rewards: head starts, your veteran on the new board, cautious multipliers, trophies and rank. Free roam back to old locations.

## Build 1: tier 1-2 vertical slice (current)

Rowboat 3x2, the captain, one rod, a cooler. The camera stays on the boat.

**Interaction model: one tap per action.** Speech bubbles float above whatever needs you; there is no walking around by hand.

**First-voyage pass (2026-10-15):** start with an optional goal of three fish
safely stowed. Hints follow the real state (cast, wait, strike, carry, return)
and can be disabled in Settings. For the first three landed fish, including
saved fish-log progress, bite waits are clamped to 4–6 seconds and the strike
window is at least 1.5 seconds. Normal timings resume afterward. Early strikes
still lose bait. There is no forced return at the three-fish goal.

Catches show their supplied portrait, weight, approximate current sale value
and new-species/record feedback. Stowing shows +1; returning shows sale coins.
The market recommends the next useful upgrade and links to the Chandlery.
The big cooler now costs **60 coins**, carrying 16 instead of 10 fish, so a full
first trip can fund storage and replacement bait. Rod holder and later prices
remain as listed in GameData. Existing owners and saves retain their upgrades.

- **Rod cycle:**
  1. Tap the **Fish** bubble above a rod. The captain walks there, baits the hook (1.2 s; skipped when the deckhand already baited it) and casts by himself.
  2. The **strike minigame** opens: a close-up of the bobber with a fish circling closer. Fish shadows also swim in the water around the boat.
  3. Wait 6-14 s through 1-3 nibbles (the bobber twitches and the fish darts in).
  4. When the bobber goes fully under, **tap anywhere once** inside the bite window (0.9 s). Tapping on a nibble spooks the fish, and missing the window lets it get away; both cost the bait.
  5. Reel (1-2.5 s by weight). The captain tosses the fish onto the deck next to him.
  6. **Solo:** tap the fish's **Carry** bubble and the captain takes it to the cooler. **With a deckhand (tier 2+):** they fetch it and bait the hook; stay in fishing mode and choose Throw again or Leave.

  A fish left on deck flops back overboard after 25 s. A rod nobody is standing at shows a **Watch** bubble (and **!** on a bite): tap it to go there.
- **Cooler:** 10 fish. Without the ice box, fish lose value the longer they sit (down to 60%).
- **Food:** bought at Supplies before sailing, in minutes. Out of food means 70% speed.
- **Bait:** worms (1 per hook) and squid (draws bigger fish and bigger species; used first when you have it).
- **Rod holder:** a second rod. A rod nobody is holding hooks a biting fish by itself 35% of the time; otherwise the fish usually keeps nibbling, and sometimes steals the bait.
- **Deckhand (tier 2):** baits hooks and carries fish to the cooler.

### Gull Harbour, the first world

The harbour is a quay with five shops. Tap a building's bubble to open it:

| Shop | What it does |
|---|---|
| Fish Market | Your last sale and today's prices (the market reacts to what you sell). Start a new game |
| Harbour Master | The sea chart (pick a sea, buy charts for new seas) and the contract board (3 offers) |
| Crew Office | Deckhand and fisherman contracts. Both roles remain available after hiring; choose any mix up to the hull's crew cap. |
| Chandlery | Rod holder, big cooler (16 fish), ice box, fibreglass rods (1.15 s strike window, faster reel), carbon rods (1.4 s, self-hook 45%), and the skiff refit goal |
| Supplies | Worms, squid, food, lunch tin |

Five seas, each with its own look and fish. Stocks drop as you fish a sea and recover every voyage.

| Sea | Chart | Row | Fish |
|---|---|---|---|
| Harbour mouth | free | 5 s | herring, mackerel |
| Kelp bank | free | 9 s | mackerel, pollock, some herring and sea bass |
| Sandbar | 120 | 11 s | plaice, mackerel, sea bass |
| Rocky point | 220 | 14 s | pollock, sea bass, mackerel, conger |
| Deep channel | 500 | 20 s | cod, conger, pollock (slow bites, bring food) |

Progress saves in the harbour (after selling and after every purchase). A voyage in progress is not saved.

Bot balance run (30 voyages):
- Sandbar chart at about 30 min
- Rocky point at about 50 min
- all gear by about 90 min
- Deep channel at about 100 min
- skiff money at about 2.5-3 hours

Earnings climb from about 25 to 40 coins per minute as seas unlock.

- **Skiff refit:** bring home 16 fish in one voyage, then pay 1,500 coins at the Chandlery. This survives saving and loading; older saves remain rowboats.

## Build 2: skiff foundation (2026-10-15)

The refit replaces the boat with the supplied skiff model and changes the walkable deck from 3×2 to 5×3. The console, hold and winch block walking; crew route around them. The skiff has three rod sockets, a 40-fish hold and an outboard that halves travel times. Crew caps are currently two on the rowboat and three on the skiff, plus the captain.

Supplies sells 10 fuel for 10 coins. A round trip consumes `ceil(spot.RowSeconds / 5)` fuel, reserved at launch, including the return. The shipyard includes the first 10 fuel in the refit price. Food capacity grows fourfold on the skiff (24 minutes, or 48 with the lunch tin), still shared by everyone aboard. The ice-box upgrade continues to preserve fish in the hold.

The rowboat, skiff and compact fisher always offer fisherman and deckhand contracts, even after hiring a worker of that role. Fishermen occupy one rod each, bait/cast autonomously, and strike 80%/85%/90% of bites by grade. Fishermen cost twice a deckhand at the same grade. They leave landed fish for deckhands. Up to three hires can be any role mix; workers leave when their 2/3/5-voyage contracts expire.

**Gill net:** tap Set net with the captain's hands free. The captain walks to the winch and sets it; it soaks for 60–90 seconds while rods keep fishing. Floats bob and the catch count rises. Tap Haul net when ready: the captain cranks for five working seconds, dropping fish onto the deck for carrying. Nets mainly catch herring/mackerel with local bycatch. Fish on deck still flop away after 25 seconds, making transport capacity matter. Returning home clears the deployed net; net fish are only sold after hauling and stowing.

The captain, deckhands and fishermen now use the delivered skinned crew and role outfits. Eleven clips cover idle, walk, carry, bait, cast, wait, strike, reel, row, cheer and stow, sampled using the simulation clock.

The bot buys, hires, fuels, sets and hauls through the same actions as the player. The final seed 7 30-voyage run reached the skiff at voyage 28; its first three voyages earned 86/133/73 gross coins per minute, versus late rowboat voyages around 30–54. Skiff voyages took 6.0/6.0/5.3 minutes, with two deck fish lost on the first haul voyage. The average meets the initial throughput improvement goal; longer voyage pacing and more seeds remain future tuning work.

Verification runs on `Catchline-sim`, never batchmode on the open main project. `docs/tools/check.ps1` syncs assets to that scratch project; use `-Method Catchline.EditorTools.SkiffChecks.Run` for refit/save/fuel/rig checks, `-Method Catchline.EditorTools.SkiffChecks.Automation` for crew/net tests, or its default method for a 30-voyage balance run. The automated checks use a separate temporary save file.

## Zero-coin contract sale fix — 2026-10-15

Every fish in a sold haul earns its normal market value immediately, including
fish matching an unfinished contract. Those fish also advance one matching
contract; completion pays the listed reward as an additional bonus. Partial or
expired contracts never consume unpaid fish. The live haul/port quote uses this
same calculation. Historical zero-payout behaviour was a bug, not intended design.

Fisherman feedback (Mo, 2026-10-15): basic/expert/veteran strike80/85/90% of bites. Automatic catches, misses, bite badges, lost-fish and stow popups stay silent; captain feedback remains visible. Aggregate haul and crew status still update.

## Mo fish/content tuning — 2026-10-16

Ten species now share the five existing grounds: whiting joins Harbour mouth and
Kelp bank, red mullet joins Sandbar and Rocky point, turbot joins Sandbar and Deep
channel. Existing species IDs are append-only for save compatibility. New
contracts use the same voyage board and known-ground pool. Pollock/bass/cod/conger
prices rise about20–29%; herring/mackerel/plaice prices stay unchanged.

Squid still adds0.4 cast power, now also suppresses herring/mackerel table weight
to4% and biases toward substantial species. Its specimen roll uses the upper65%
of each species' weight range. This improves expected landed value; missed
strikes, fish availability, freshness and market saturation still matter. No
guaranteed profit per cast. Scraps remain unlimited and restricted to small
herring/mackerel. Worms remain the everyday low-cost option.

Supplies also offers30 worms for24 coins and15 squid for36, alongside existing
10-for10 and5-for15 packs. The existing Use squid first toggle remains a preference;
when worms run out, remaining squid is used. Fishing HUD now names the next bait.
Catch portraits are rendered from distinct authored fish models; rewards retain
nonblocking captain-only feedback. Native Unity art/HUD/audio review is pending.
See CLI-FISH-CONTENT-REVIEW.md for measured checks and integration notes.
