# Tier 3 progression, nets and launchers — NET023

Mo approved a100kg tier3 hold (150kg with existing BigCooler), equal10kg shallow/
deep net limits and a5000coin Heavy net system to raise both limits to15kg.

## Catch and economy

Net generation ends at remaining kilograms; there is no5fish limit or hidden64fish
cap. Every specimen stays inside its local species' weight range. Deep nets favour
large specimens with greater per-kg prices, then fill residual space with local
school fish. Normal nets take many small school fish. Both obey the same hard mass
ceiling. Richness can reduce realized yield; equipment capacity stays10/15kg.
Individual hold storage retains the approved single-whole-fish final overflow rule;
net generation itself cannot exceed its gear limit. No fake weight clipping of fish.

3–4large fish is typical where local sizes support it, not a forced fish count.
The harbour has small species; the deep channel has much heavier cod/eels. Actual
200seed distribution measurements and100kg illustrative sales are in screens/net023.

Only tier3 now depletes grounds by weight (.0035richness/kg rather than per fish)
and uses5kg as the market-pressure reference rather than.5kg. Other boats retain
starter balance. Sale preview copies hull as well as market/upgrades, so actual
payout follows the same calculation. Freshness and contract bonuses are unchanged.

## Upgrades

One authoritative GameState.UpgradeCost drives purchasing, price labels,
affordability, shortfalls and shop-news checks. Existing owned IDs remain owned.
No repurchase charge is invented at refit. New NetSystem ID appends to saved enum.

| Tier3 upgrade | Coins |
|---|---:|
| Extra fuel tank |1500|
| Fibreglass rods |1800|
| Ice box |2500|
| Big cooler:100→150kg |3000|
| Carbon rods (requires fibreglass) |3500|
| Heavy net system:10→15kg |5000|

Powered boats already have their rod sockets, so redundant rodholder purchase is
hidden. Starter prices remain intact. Real games can buy the Compact fisher from
an owned skiff in Chandlery for4000coins; it retains crew/upgrades/cargo and includes
its first tank. It is no longer reachable only through the test fixture.

These are initial tuning targets, not measured1–3trip promises. Illustrative100kg
fresh sales:1333small/1881halfsmall-halfbig; all aged3minutes:1111/1552. Example
expenses245/332 leave866/1220. Composition, freshness, skill, net failure, bait,
crew grade and previous market sales change actual profit. The explicit5000net
upgrade is a longer investment than ordinary upgrades.

## Real game and test game

Separate Windows scenes/builds serialize SessionOnly=false for Real and true for
Test before Awake/Build. Test never loads, writes or deletes the real save. Shortcut
flags cannot turn it into a real game. Real uses the existing persistent path under
AppData/LocalLow/DefaultCompany/Catchline/catchline-save.json and loads existing
progress. Harbour purchases, arrival and sale save progress; a voyage in progress
still follows existing quit/lose-trip messaging. Return home before quitting to
preserve your fish. No test money/crew/net stock appears in real progression.

Stable installation: C:/Users/momoy/Projects/Catchline-play/Real and /Test.
Desktop shortcuts: Catchline - Real Game and Catchline - Tier 3 Test. Real launches
normal title/continue at native display. At NET023 delivery, Test launched an
isolated tier3,200coins,threecrew,60worms,fullfuel and deep3uses+reserve. CREW026
supersedes that default Test setup with an empty-roster hiring sandbox; the
at-sea setup remains available through `-deep-net-trial`.

## Verification

check-net023.ps1 runs dedicated scratch. New native progression tests verify both
net caps and local weight limits over4000hauls, deepvalue/schoolcount differences,
exact5000shop purchase, upgrade save/reload, kgdepletion and quote=payout. Existing
fuel and full weight/net/catch/crew/chart/clearance regressions run again. Actual
Canvas/world captures include upgrade purchase controls and a many-fish stored haul.

Built standalone probes use an explicit temporary net023-probe-* root only in
batchmode: separate Real processes write/reload coins, hull, net upgrade and unsold
fish; Test starts with that populated file and must ignore/preserve it. The actual
captain save hash is checked unchanged. All diagnostic players stop after testing.
Shortcuts are read back through Windows Shell and installed exe hashes compared.
Native screenshots/control invocation are not physical phone/pointer acceptance.

# Fuel and departure — FUEL021

Mo's16October update combines WEIGHT020 storage/pricing/deep nets, CATCH018 rewards,
CHART017 illustrated chart and CREW016 character polish with fuel and preparation.

## Rules

Early boats have no active food purchase, consumption or hunger penalty. Existing
crew/action work runs at the former70%pace as its baseline. A future cook/food bonus
is a future feature; no cook is sold or invented in this update. Powered travel keeps
its engine timing; manual rowing follows the crew baseline.

Powered boats have10minutes of fuel,20with the100coin extra-tank upgrade. The old
LunchTin enum/owned-save ID is deliberately retained and now grants tank capacity.
No player needs to repurchase an already owned upgrade. Existing legacy food fields
are dormant save/fixture data; FillFood cannot charge coins.

Fuel burns at1minute per actual simulated minute during outward travel, fishing and
return, independent of crew count. Pausing stops simulation. At10%fuel while fishing,
the boat immediately heads home, using the existing departure handling: carried/deck
fish are stowed individually as space permits, unstored overflow and deployed catch
are forfeited. The final10%is reserved for the home leg:1minute on the base tank,
2minutes with the upgrade. Existing return travel is shorter than either reserve.
There is no dock refill. Fuel persists as fractional minutes and is shown as percent.

Refilling costs1coin per missing minute, rounded up, matching the former provision
unit cost. Full/insufficient-funds purchases never charge. First shipyard refit includes
its first tank. The rowboat remains oar-powered and needs no fuel. Old numeric fuel
loads into the new minutes field and is clamped to the owned tank capacity after
loading upgrades. Empty fuel never silently refills on save/load.

## Ready to leave

Set sail opens a native coastal manifest; only Let's go starts the trip. The manifest
shows destination, live coins, fuel percent/tank, worms/squid, free scraps, actual crew
and contract voyages, selected net and stock/remaining uses. Refuel and bait controls
buy actual supplies at the same shop prices and save normal games. Disabled full,
unaffordable and no-fuel departure states are explicit. Back/Escape returns to port.
Crew hiring, upgrades and net purchases/loadout remain in the real harbour shops.

## Verification and release

Runner: docs/tools/check-fuel021.ps1. Uses independent Catchline-rewards-sim; never
batchmodes main. All native test fixtures/builds are SessionOnly and cannot read or

# CREW026 — Always-available contract hiring

Mo's clarified request: keep contract hiring temporary, but do not let a worker
type disappear from the Crew Office after the available candidate is hired.

## Rules

- Real and Test maintain the same role availability.
- The Crew Office always offers enough deckhand contracts and fisherman
  contracts to fill the current hull's full crew capacity. Hiring one candidate
  immediately replenishes that role with another candidate; board rolls, refits
  and loading older saves restore the full choices too.
- Tier3 supports three fishermen, three deckhands or any mix. The rowboat keeps
  its two existing hired berths; the captain remains additional.
- Hiring is still a temporary contract. Hands last2 voyages, experienced crew3
  and veterans5. Existing grade fees and the fisherman's2x fee remain unchanged;
  expired crew leave normally and must be hired again.
- Full crew does not remove the captain's own work.

## Test setup

The `Catchline - Tier 3 Test` desktop shortcut starts at the Crew Office with an
empty compact-fisher roster and1000 session-only coins. That allows three
veteran fishermen (900 coins) as well as cheaper mixes. Normal contract fees and
durations apply. The test has full fuel and the existing bait/net supplies; it
never reads or writes the Real save.

The previous at-sea setup remains available by launching the Test player with
`-deep-net-trial -native-display`. The Real shortcut continues normal title/load
and persistent saves; no test currency or crew is added to Real progression.

## Verification

`UrgentCrewChecks.Run` covers each hull and30 board seeds, all-fisher/
all-deckhand/mixed compositions, replacement offers after each hire, contract
timers through save/load and expiry, and actual Test Crew Office purchase
controls at desktop and phone sizes. `ProgressionLaunchChecks.ValidateAndBuild`
runs the integrated regression, water check, and separate Real/Test Windows
builds on `Catchline-sim`. The guarded Real standalone probe checks both role
lists under a temporary `net023-probe-*` save root; it does not target the
player's save.

Delivered builds and shortcuts are versioned under
`Catchline-play/Crew026/{Real,Test}`. Shortcut readback and installed-file hashes
are recorded in `Catchline-sim/Logs/crew026/shortcuts.json`; the full verification
and correction history is in `comms.md`.

# Weight storage, specimen pricing and deep nets

Codex 1 (Windows app), 2026-10-16. Current implementation supersedes NET015's
count-based cargo/yield and 80-coin shallow replacement tuning. Pickup history,
ownership and exact verification boundaries are in `comms.md`, WEIGHT020 A–G.

## Player rules

| Hull | Base hold | With the existing Big cooler upgrade |
|---|---:|---:|
| Rowboat | 4 kg | 6 kg |
| Skiff | 18 kg | 27 kg |
| Compact fisher | 24 kg | 36 kg |

Big cooler still costs 60 coins, preserves its existing saved upgrade ID, and now
adds 50% capacity on every hull. Hull IDs and crew/grid layouts are unchanged.
Kilograms are fish cargo capacity, not total vessel displacement or crew weight.

Any whole fish can enter while the stored mass is strictly below capacity. That
fish may overfill the hold by any amount; further inserts are rejected. A net batch
is processed fish by fish, not treated as one fish. Captain/crew deliveries recheck
at their actual storage callback, so simultaneous animations never grant two final
spaces. Remaining basket fish are retained, even if the fitted net used its last
use. Returning warns about the same carried-first/deck-order overflow that the
actual departure will discard; there is no new automatic forced return.

HUD, chart, world hold label, shop and storage upgrade wording use kg. Individual
fish counts remain meaningful for the journal, contracts, haul receipts and the
existing sixteen-fish refit milestone; those are not hidden cargo slot caps.

## Specimen value and market

Each species has a base rate. Its relative specimen size in its authored min/max
range increases that rate smoothly from 1x at minimum size to 1.5x at maximum.
The premium is capped, including oversized fish from old saves. Thus the same
mass of larger specimens earns more than smaller specimens of that species.
Species identity still matters; not every large cheap species beats every small
premium species in every market.

Base coins/kg in saved species order: Herring12, Mackerel13, Pollock15, Sea bass19,
Conger18, Plaice16, Cod17, Whiting14, Red mullet18, Turbot22. Existing sale rounding,
freshness and contract rewards apply after specimen pricing. Quote and actual
sale/receipt paths share `ValueOf`; the market catalogue labels its base rate
explicitly and mentions the size bonus.

Market pressure now scales with sold kg: each half kilogram applies the previous
0.97 price multiplier. Existing market recovery and 50% floor remain. This avoids
charging much more saturation for the same weight of small fish. Stock depletion
still counts catches; journal and contract quantities retain individual fish.

## Net preparation and wear

Supplies on the compact fisher has two net product cards and a loadout selector
at the top. Selection is harbour-only. Inventory is saved independently:

| Method | Purchase | Lifetime | Soak | Catch direction |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| Shallow | 30 coins | Reusable; tear destroys one | 20 s | Local schooling species; smaller specimens |
| Deep | 100 coins | 3 uses per net | 30 s | Local full species pool; larger specimens |

Shallow stock still starts at three including the fitted net, rack limit four.
Deep stock starts at zero, rack limit three including fitted. Every deep-net entry
stores its remaining uses; exhausted fitted nets rotate to the next reserve.

A completed deep haul spends one use whether it succeeds or fails. Failure loses
that catch, not the other uses or reserves. A failed setting minigame also spends
one use. Clean setting and cancellation spend none. Abandoning a soaking/ready net
forfeits its unlanded catch through the existing departure confirmation; it awards
no fish and does not refund a previously spent use. No automatic net-type fallback.

Captain setting and hauling retain the existing tap-only skill games and two
cumulative mistakes. There is no random captain failure. A worker has exactly an
80% successful recovery chance, rolled once at completion; a failed shallow haul
destroys one shallow net, a failed deep haul spends one deep use. Existing Fish/Nets
duties, concurrent actor passage and automatic whole-batch unloading remain.

Catch rewards are mass budgets, not guaranteed fish counts. Ground-specific mass
uses the local species table's average weights; deep target mass is roughly twice
shallow. Depletion reduces the target. Only local species are rolled; deep weighting
favours heavier local species and sizes in the top35% of their range, shallow sizes
in the lower10–50%. Whole final fish can exceed a net's mass target. A 64-fish safety
limit bounds malformed future content; it is not the normal reward rule. No claim
that a deep net always gives twice the number of fish, or guarantees rare species.

## Balance evidence and limitations

Production code was sampled across 500 seeds per ground and method (5,000 hauls).
Values below are fresh, initially unsaturated sale quotes including within-haul
market impact. They exclude fuel, food, crew, travel/handling time and losses from
existing market saturation, freshness, hold overflow or player mistakes.

| Ground | Shallow mean kg / gross coins | Deep mean kg / gross coins |
|---|---:|---:|
| Harbour mouth | 1.81 / 26.74 | 3.56 / 64.18 |
| Kelp bank | 2.96 / 47.88 | 5.95 / 127.07 |
| Sandbar | 3.47 / 47.63 | 7.79 / 200.74 |
| Rocky point | 5.99 / 91.74 | 13.14 / 304.84 |
| Deep channel | 10.51 / 140.14 | 22.80 / 494.42 |

At Harbour mouth, a worker's expected contribution after net expense is about
15.40 coins per shallow attempt and 18.01 per deep attempt, before voyage costs.
Deep's longer cycle makes shallow competitive there. On later grounds deep nets
are substantial money makers; a deep-channel haul can fill the compact hold alone,
so running rods concurrently creates overflow rather than unlimited extra income.
These are first-pass balance targets, not measured human profit/minute or a claim
that every loadout/ground/market is profitable. See `docs/screens/weight020/balance.csv`.

## Source and migration

- `VoyageStorage`: actual mass/fullness/atomic insertion, no cached count budget.
- `GameState` / `GameData`: kg capacities, upgrade, specimen prices, market exponent,
  additive save fields with existing version1 and stable upgrade/species/hull IDs.
- `GameStateNetLoadout`: selected method, purchase, per-net uses and reserve rotation.
- `VoyageNetLoadout`: harbour preflight and local mass range; `VoyageNet` integrates
  cycle ownership/debits/yield, preserving authored stern float/rope visuals.
- `VoyageNetUnload` / `VoyageGame` / `VoyageHaul`: stow and departure order/capacity.
- Existing HUD/chart/shop/goal components show real state using supplied artwork.

Older saves retain each fish's actual weight, unsold cargo, owned cooler, money,
crew, charts and market state. Missing deep fields mean no deep stock and shallow
selection. Explicit exhausted zero remains zero; list entries are clamped/sanitized
on load. No existing cargo is truncated to the new limit. Saves happen at harbour
as before; this does not add in-voyage resume.

## Checks and preview

`WeightNetChecks.Regression` runs the focused migration/storage/pricing/deep-net
tests plus the full prior first-voyage, skiff, automation, UI, tap-net, inventory,
crew, catch reward, illustrated chart and all-map clearance regression suites.
Native captures include real world+Canvas at1440x900 and844x390, actual supplies
loadout and scrolled deep purchase. Physical touch and mobile GPU profiling remain
unverified. Existing secondary phone typography and broader content balance remain
separate work.

Separate SessionOnly preview: `Catchline-sim/Builds/Weight020/Catchline.exe`.
Explicit `-deep-net-trial` starts the compact boat/200coins/three hired crew with
two full deep nets and deep selected. These are isolated test supplies, not gifts
to normal saves. `-native-display` requests the laptop's borderless native display.
No foreground launch is part of this implementation task.

Conditions, seasons, ranks and journal expansion remain separate design work.

# Catch presentation and stored-net receipts — Codex 2 (WSL CLI)

Mo requested a finished catch presentation with subtle rarity backlights and a
species list when net fish reach storage. This replaces the plain catch portrait
panel with a navy specimen display and a matching stored-haul manifest.

| Current tier | Backlight | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Common | Soft white | Normal common specimen |
| Common · big | Green | Large common specimen |
| Rare | Blue | Normal rare specimen |
| Rare · big | Purple | Large rare specimen |
| Legendary | Gold | Style reserved for future legendary fish |
| Mythical | Red | Style reserved for future massive legendary fish |

Current rare presentation species: sea bass, conger eel and turbot. Other current
species are common. “Big” begins at 75% of each species' existing min–max weight
range, so a large mackerel can be green despite weighing less than a normal cod.
This is presentation metadata, independent of market price, catch probability,
location, save IDs or actual rarity odds. No existing fish becomes legendary or
mythical; the two additional visual styles are ready for later special content.

The native Canvas graphic draws a rounded navy enamel surface and a soft radial
backlight behind the existing transparent fish artwork. There is no generated
replacement fish art, bloom, extra camera, or shared Canvas-source modification.
Catch cards show species, weight, estimated current value, rarity words and the
existing new-species/personal-best feedback. They last six simulation seconds,
with a brief settling movement. Captain feedback remains separate from automatic
fisher catches, which preserve the existing quiet feedback behavior.

Net receipts appear only after the actual storage insert. They list species and
counts, total weight and estimated value. Desktop rows also show each species'
value; compact phone rows prioritize species/count and weight. The footer shows
the whole stored batch. The manifest remains visible for eleven simulation
seconds, followed by a “Last net” review action. Reviewing is read-only: it does
not store fish again, award money, update records or consume random numbers.

Snapshots include only successfully inserted fish. An interrupted gather or
stow creates no false receipt. A partly full hold reports only the fish it
accepted; the leftover basket remains unstored. Manually storing a leftover net
fish also produces a truthful one-fish receipt. Empty callbacks do not overwrite
a previous receipt. New voyages clear the ephemeral receipt; no save migration
or persistent catch data is changed.

## Files and verification

Runtime: `CatchPresentation.cs`, `CatchRewardGraphic.cs`,
`VoyageCatchRewards.cs`, plus narrow hooks in `VoyageFeedback.cs`,
`VoyageInterface.cs`, `VoyageNetUnload.cs` and `VoyageGame.TryStow`.

`CatchRewardChecks` exercises active tier boundaries, no current gold/red fish,
actual set/soak/haul/gather/stow reporting, exact species/count/weight/value,
cancellation, full/partial/manual storage safety, snapshot immutability,
read-only review, RNG isolation and native viewport/control clearance.

Native captures cover white/green/blue/purple, desktop and landscape phone,
net manifest, larger type and review state. `net-list-*` are explicitly authored
presentation fixtures using five real supported fish through the production
snapshot/render methods; the actual net distribution/storage cycle is exercised
separately in the executable checks. They are Unity world plus production Canvas
captures, not browser mockups or a rendered-player input test.

Codex 1 concurrently owns CHART017 and the original Catchline-sim. This pass uses
an independent `Catchline-rewards-sim`, keeping that scratch and its held chart,
net-visual and shared Canvas paths untouched. Run:

```powershell
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File docs/tools/check-catch018.ps1
```

The runner syncs only the main Assets/Packages/ProjectSettings into the dedicated
scratch and runs hidden Unity, the existing first-voyage/net/economy/crew
regressions, the focused catch checks, native captures and a separate build.
Output: `Catchline-rewards-sim/Builds/Catch018/Catchline.exe`.
Use `-netboat-trial -native-display` for the existing isolated 200-coin boat trial.
No main-project batchmode, foreground restart, commit, staging or reset.

Physical playtest feel and Mo's visual acceptance remain user checks. Gold/red
are style support only. Existing small secondary HUD text and overlapping world
action bubbles remain outside this catch-presentation scope.

Final verification: `check-catch018-check.log` exited 0 with all listed
regressions, focused checks, captures and Windows build completed. Native
captures were inspected; no material scoped visual defect remained. The built
player passed a bounded headless isolated-startup check without runtime
exceptions/readability/Graphic errors; the diagnostic alone was stopped.
Evidence, exact verified source hashes and native images are under
`docs/screens/catch018/`. The dedicated build contains its captured main-source
snapshot; Codex 1 must re-sync released CATCH018 sources into any subsequent
CHART017 build before claiming those features integrated.

# Catchline — next boat and small net

Mo's current scope, 2026-10-16: **3D first; V1 size approved, finished model delivered in ART-022.**
The rowboat is tier 1; the new compact motor fisher is tier 2 in Mo's latest
wording. These display tiers do not renumber saved `Hull` IDs. Earlier zero-based
planning remains history; do not migrate progression from names alone.

## First model / ART-021

- [x] Mo reviewed/approved V1 hull size, helm roof and four-person deck against the rowboat.
- [x] ART-022 finishes the approved silhouette without enlargement: 5.4m hull,
  2.8m beam, compared with the 4.2m/2.4m rowboat; open sides, roof over helm only,
  small rear net. Real hold opening, independent lid/drum/crank and fittings added.
  Source/actual FBX/static access checked; native gameplay integration remains below.
  See [finished 3D handoff](docs/CLI-COMPACT-FISHER-022.md).
- [ ] Codex 1 checks native import axes/materials, camera framing, four crew routes,
  rod access, helm/hold access, net handling and travel/docking clearances before
  selecting this model in gameplay. V1 is a separate asset, not a Skiff replacement.
- [ ] Settle whether this tier carries captain + three hired crew immediately, and
  how flexible roles/coverage warnings are presented. Four-person model capacity
  does not itself change hiring rules or introduce a net-operator role.

## Proposed small-net loop — NOT IMPLEMENTED / values to playtest

Use a **small stationary set net**, not a giant trawl: set it off the stern,
fish with the rods while it soaks, haul it, then carry/stow its catch. No bait is
required. The tradeoffs are time, handling work, shared hold space and **net wear**.
Keeping this first net stationary leaves steering/tow management for a later boat.

- [ ] Start tuning at 2 seconds setting + 90 seconds soaking + 5 seconds hauling,
  plus walking/carrying. One active net; no idle/offline earnings. Pause freezes it.
- [ ] Try **2–5 fish per completed haul**, probabilities 20%/40%/30%/10% (mean3.3).
  Prioritize local small schooling fish, with limited local bycatch; do not make
  this tiny net an easy source of trophy fish. Final per-location tables need tests.
- [ ] Keep bait relevance: rods using squid should remain the route to valuable
  large fish. Net catches must not inherit the captain's paid-bait multiplier.
- [ ] Prototype **100% condition, 12 percentage points wear per completed haul**.
  Eight full hauls leave4%; require at least12% to set again. Show the condition
  and repair cost before departure/setting. Never randomly destroy a net/catch.
- [ ] Try repair at the existing Chandlery: `ceil(16 * damage / 100)` coins to
  restore to100%. Eight hauls cost16 coins to restore96% damage, effectively2/haul.
  No extra currency or separate repair screen. Do not implement these prices yet.
- [ ] Keep it recoverable: no coins/condition means net unavailable with a clear
  repair reason; free unlimited scraps and ordinary rod fishing remain available.
  Net repair is optional, not a departure requirement or a new soft lock.
- [ ] Charge wear once on successful haul; save/load or canceled walks must not
  duplicate catch, refund an already delivered haul, or charge twice. Decide and
  explain returning with a soaking/ready net; recommend recover it before departure,
  with an explicit forfeiture confirmation if leaving early, never silent loss.
- [ ] Capacity preview: show expected catch range and remaining hold space before
  setting. At haul, use existing deck backlog/carrying/freshness rules; no instant
  sale and no silent overflow deletion. Catch age starts when the haul lands.
- [ ] Native tests: empty/full hold, full deck, no deckhand, two fishers/no hand,
  paused soak, save/reload, canceled set/haul, low condition/zero money, departure
  during soak, sale preview/payout equality, and deterministic yield across seeds.

### Starting economics, not a measured balance result

Example harbour table60% herring/40% mackerel at current mean weights/prices:
`0.6*(0.21*17) + 0.4*(0.5*14) = 4.942 coins/fish` before freshness/market effects.
Expected haul `3.3*4.942 = 16.3086` gross coins. Ignoring walking/carrying,97seconds
is about10.09 gross coins/min; subtract2 coins wear reserve per haul for about
8.85 coins/min. These are illustrative proposals, not actual runtime yield.
Native balance must include deckhand throughput, provision/fuel costs, location
depletion, market changes and opportunity cost of the captain's handling time.

The existing `VoyageNet.cs` is much more generous: first catch after4seconds,
then every3–4.5seconds scaled by richness during a60–90second soak, with30% spot
bycatch and no wear. Do not mistake that old prototype for this proposed tiny-net
balance; replace it in a separate scoped systems pass after the model review.

## Keep later boats interesting — suggestions, not approved expansion

- [ ] Explore crab/lobster pots: bait, placement decisions and collection routes.
- [ ] Explore a later purpose-built trawler: steer through schools, manage tow speed
  and load. This is different work, not simply a bigger version of the first net.
- [ ] Decide later activities before escalating hull size. No cabin, industrial
  gantry, cargo-scale hold or new fishing system is required for this V1.

## Gameplay ideas — journal and deep nets

Ideas Mo asked to keep for later design work. Weight capacity and the deep-net
failure rule are now approved direction in docs/GAMEPLAY.md (2026-10-16), not
implemented. Other proposals and all example balance values remain unsettled.

### Fish journal and massive-catch achievements

- [ ] Add a fish journal that records catches by species, including personal size
  records and notable milestones.
- [ ] Explore species-specific achievements or special rewards for massive
  catches. Define what counts as massive separately for each fish type; thresholds
  and repeat rewards are undecided.
- [ ] Consider collection rewards such as journal marks, harbour trophies or boat
  cosmetics, so records have value beyond ordinary sale income. Exact rewards and
  whether any reward is repeatable remain undecided.

### Deep nets

- [x] WEIGHT020 implements approved weight-based storage/net direction: kg capacity upgrades,
  higher value per kg for large specimens, and one final whole fish accepted
  while the hold is below capacity. A failed deep haul loses catch and one use,
  keeping remaining uses; carry reserve deep nets. Current values,5000seed native
  mass/value sample and remaining play-balance limits are in docs/WEIGHT020.md.

- [ ] Explore a costly, limited-use deep net that shifts catch odds toward the
  better fish available in the selected map. It must not introduce fish from
  outside that map's species pool.
- [x] WEIGHT020 starts at100coins per net and3uses, including failed hauls.
  The prior4–5fish/2greatfish hypothesis is superseded by local weight budgets and
  larger-specimen bias; rare species are not guaranteed. Future tuning stays open.
- [ ] Make the net clearly worthwhile after its purchase cost, time and handling
  work. Compare its expected sale value and journal/record value with ordinary
  nets and rods across maps, including depleted grounds and market effects.
- [ ] Work out how skill affects the limited uses, what happens when a haul is
  interrupted or the hold is full, and how much the better-fish odds improve.
  Preserve fair, predictable results; do not promise a positive payout until the
  balance is tested.

## Deferred — modular chum station (Mo, 2026-10-17)

**To do for a larger tier 4 or 5 boat; not for implementation now.** Exact
unlock tier remains undecided. This is a future production link between catches,
crew and rod fishing, rather than another shop-bought bait upgrade.

- [ ] Fit a chum-making station into a modular boat slot. It trades equipment
  space and crew time for top-tier bait; larger boats must have room/personnel
  to support it. Exact footprint and slot compatibility remain to be designed.
- [ ] Let the player assign a specific hand to chum production instead of net
  work or collecting fishermen's catches, using a simple RimWorld-like work order.
- [ ] Support a bounded order such as **make 10 chum**, then stop. Avoid requiring
  a tap for every portion. Whether a later repeat/maintain-stock option is useful
  remains open; it is not required for this first version.
- [ ] Require caught fish as input, consuming eligible fish to produce chum.
  Fish-to-chum yield and processing time are undecided.
- [ ] Let the player choose an input limit by fish value or weight: for example,
  **only fish worth under 10 coins** or **only fish under 1 kg**. These are example
  player settings, not fixed balance values. Define the value basis and whether
  both filters can be combined before implementation; protect ineligible fish.
- [ ] Chum is the top-tier bait for attracting the biggest fish. It cannot be
  purchased: earn it from the voyage's catch and processing work each run.
  Exact attraction/size effects and consumption per cast remain untuned.
- [ ] Chum spoils and does not carry over into the next voyage. Exact spoil timing
  within a voyage remains open; do not silently choose a timer now.
- [ ] Keep the first interaction compact: assign worker, set quantity, choose fish
  limit, see progress and why production is waiting. Define input pickup/storage,
  rod bait selection and worker behaviour after completion before building.

No current boat, crew role, bait, save or economy change is authorized by this
backlog entry. Revisit alongside tier 4/5 modular-boat design.

## Tier 4 prep/chum station — current direction (2026-10-17)

See [BOAT032 design and model comparison](docs/TIER4-BLOCKOUT032.md). This refines
and supersedes the earlier tier4/5 chum-only station proposal above. Both rough
model variants are rendered and ready for Mo's review; no gameplay system is implemented.

- [ ] One dual-purpose table, up to two assigned ordinary deckhands, plus optional
  captain help through a quick green-zone head-cutting minigame.
- [ ] Drag job priorities; maintain10chum in stock, then prepare eligible fish;
  replenish chum when consumed if suitable input fish exists. Fish input limits
  by value/weight and preparation species/minimum-weight selection remain part
  of the design. Chum remains earned per voyage and unpurchasable.
- [ ] Local flow: raw front buckets -> table -> prepared freezer storage directly
  behind each hand. Minimal depositing travel and only representative visible fill.
- [ ] Prepared fish NEVER spoils. Untreated fish in ordinary front buckets still
  spoils. Upgrade front buckets to ice coolers to protect untreated fish as well.
- [ ] Aim for quick processing alongside catching; fuel limits optional waiting
  at voyage end. Approximately25%prepared sale premium remains to be tuned.
- [ ] Review compact larger hull before production modelling: initial6.2x3.1m
  candidate was too small for Mo; current comparison6.8x3.5m against actual
  tier3 5.4x2.8m, identical crew scale. Final size/crew cap not approved yet.

using UnityEngine;

namespace Catchline.Game
{
    // References to the imported models (filled by Catchline > Rebuild Art Library).
    // Anything left empty falls back to the primitive placeholder.
    [CreateAssetMenu(menuName = "Catchline/Art Library")]
    public class ArtLibrary : ScriptableObject
    {
        public GameObject Rowboat, FishingRod, Bobber, Cooler, IceBox, BaitBucket, LunchTin;
        public GameObject Quay, QuayLadder, Bollard, LampPost, HouseA, HouseB, Lighthouse, RockA, RockB, RockC, Kelp;
        public GameObject[] Fish = new GameObject[7]; // indexed by Species
        public Texture2D[] Ui;                         // Codex's UI kit, looked up by file name
        public Font UiFont;
        public GameObject[] Models;                    // every FBX under Assets/Art/Models, looked up by file name
        public AnimationClip[] CrewClips;
        public Shader WaterShader;
        public Texture2D WaterRipples, WaterCrossing;
        public Texture2D PtsWaterNormals, PtsWaterDither;
        public Texture2D[] WaterTextures;
        public Texture2D WaterTex(string name) => WaterTextures == null ? null : System.Array.Find(WaterTextures, t => t != null && t.name == name);

        System.Collections.Generic.Dictionary<string, Texture2D> uiByName;
        System.Collections.Generic.Dictionary<string, GameObject> modelByName;

        // Any model by its FBX file name ("Barrel", "ShopFront_Chandlery"); null when Codex hasn't delivered it yet.
        public GameObject M(string name)
        {
            if (modelByName == null)
            {
                modelByName = new();
                if (Models != null)
                    foreach (var m in Models)
                        if (m != null) modelByName[m.name] = m;
            }
            return modelByName.TryGetValue(name, out var go) ? go : null;
        }

        public Texture2D UiTex(string name)
        {
            if (uiByName == null)
            {
                uiByName = new();
                if (Ui != null)
                    foreach (var t in Ui)
                        if (t != null) uiByName[t.name] = t;
            }
            return uiByName.TryGetValue(name, out var tex) ? tex : null;
        }

        static ArtLibrary cached;
        static bool looked;

        public static ArtLibrary Get()
        {
            if (!looked || cached == null)
            {
                looked = true;
                cached = Resources.Load<ArtLibrary>("ArtLibrary");
            }
            return cached;
        }

        public GameObject FishFor(Species s) => Fish != null && (int)s < Fish.Length ? Fish[(int)s] : null;

        public static GameObject Spawn(GameObject prefab, Transform parent, Vector3 localPos, Quaternion localRot, float scale = 1f)
        {
            if (prefab == null) return null;
            var go = Instantiate(prefab, parent, false);
            go.name = prefab.name;
            go.transform.localPosition = localPos;
            go.transform.localRotation = localRot;
            go.transform.localScale = Vector3.one * scale;
            foreach (var c in go.GetComponentsInChildren<Collider>()) Prototype.Visuals.Kill(c);
            RemapMaterials(go);
            return go;
        }

        // Codex names every material after a STYLE.md palette colour. Swap them for the game's own URP
        // materials so models look right regardless of FBX import settings or import order.
        static readonly System.Collections.Generic.Dictionary<string, string> PaletteHex = new()
        {
            ["M_HullNavy"] = "173F57", ["M_SafetyOrange"] = "E36A35", ["M_RailIvory"] = "F3F0DC", ["M_Rope"] = "D1AF76",
            ["M_Steel"] = "8FA6AC", ["M_Rubber"] = "28343B", ["M_SeaTeal"] = "168F9F", ["M_BandIvory"] = "F3DEA1",
            ["M_DeckWood"] = "C58B50", ["M_WoodHighlight"] = "DFB77A", ["M_WoodRecess"] = "8C5A36", ["M_HullBlue"] = "2A6685",
            ["M_OilskinYellow"] = "E6AD38", ["M_Skin"] = "B9784B", ["M_KelpGreen"] = "477A56", ["M_FishSilver"] = "BAC9C6",
            ["M_HairChestnut"] = "50392E", ["M_HairSilver"] = "ADB6AF",
            ["M_CoastStone"] = "A99A7C", ["M_CoastStoneLight"] = "C9B89A", ["M_CoastWet"] = "696F64",
            ["M_CoastGrass"] = "729053", ["M_CoastGrassLight"] = "98A96B", ["M_CoastSand"] = "D9C393", ["M_SeabedSand"] = "BEA77D",
        };

        static void RemapMaterials(GameObject go)
        {
            foreach (var r in go.GetComponentsInChildren<Renderer>())
            {
                var mats = r.sharedMaterials;
                for (int i = 0; i < mats.Length; i++)
                {
                    if (mats[i] == null)
                    {
                        mats[i] = Prototype.Visuals.Mat(Palette.Steel);
                        continue;
                    }
                    string n = mats[i].name.Replace(" (Instance)", "");
                    int m = n.IndexOf("M_", System.StringComparison.Ordinal);
                    if (m > 0) n = n.Substring(m); // imported as "Rowboat-M_DeckWood"
                    int dot = n.IndexOf('.');
                    if (dot > 0) n = n.Substring(0, dot); // Blender duplicates: M_Rope.001
                    if (PaletteHex.TryGetValue(n, out var hex)) mats[i] = Prototype.Visuals.Mat(Palette.Hex(hex));
                }
                r.sharedMaterials = mats;
            }
        }

        // Points a long model (like the rod) from its pivot along a local direction with a given length,
        // whatever axis it was modelled along: the longest side of its bounds is taken as "along".
        public static void AimAlong(GameObject go, Vector3 localDir, float length)
        {
            go.transform.localRotation = Quaternion.identity;
            go.transform.localScale = Vector3.one;
            // Bounds of all meshes in the model's own space (pure math: no dependency on renderer updates).
            var toLocal = go.transform.worldToLocalMatrix;
            bool any = false;
            var b = new Bounds();
            foreach (var mf in go.GetComponentsInChildren<MeshFilter>())
            {
                if (mf.sharedMesh == null) continue;
                var m = toLocal * mf.transform.localToWorldMatrix;
                var mb = mf.sharedMesh.bounds;
                for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++)
                {
                    var corner = mb.center + Vector3.Scale(mb.extents, new Vector3((i & 1) == 0 ? -1 : 1, (i & 2) == 0 ? -1 : 1, (i & 4) == 0 ? -1 : 1));
                    var p = m.MultiplyPoint3x4(corner);
                    if (!any) { b = new Bounds(p, Vector3.zero); any = true; }
                    else b.Encapsulate(p);
                }
            }
            if (!any) return;
            var ext = b.extents;
            Vector3 axis = ext.x >= ext.y && ext.x >= ext.z ? Vector3.right : ext.y >= ext.z ? Vector3.up : Vector3.forward;
            if (Vector3.Dot(b.center, axis) < 0f) axis = -axis; // the long side runs away from the pivot (the butt)
            float size = 2f * Vector3.Dot(ext, new Vector3(Mathf.Abs(axis.x), Mathf.Abs(axis.y), Mathf.Abs(axis.z)));
            go.transform.localRotation = Quaternion.FromToRotation(axis, localDir.normalized);
            if (size > 0.01f) go.transform.localScale = Vector3.one * (length / size);
        }

        public static Transform Find(GameObject root, string name)
        {
            if (root == null) return null;
            foreach (var t in root.GetComponentsInChildren<Transform>(true))
                if (t.name == name) return t;
            return null;
        }
    }
}

FILE: Assets/Scripts/Game/CatchPresentation.cs
========================================================================
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using UnityEngine;

namespace Catchline.Game
{
    public enum CatchTier { Common, BigCommon, Rare, BigRare, Legendary, Mythical }

    public static class CatchPresentation
    {
        // Presentation metadata only: catch tables, prices and saved species IDs stay intact.
        public static bool IsRare(Species species) => species is Species.SeaBass or Species.Conger or Species.Turbot;
        public static bool IsBig(Species species, float kg)
        {
            var fish = GameData.Get(species);
            return kg >= Mathf.Lerp(fish.MinKg, fish.MaxKg, .75f);
        }
        public static CatchTier Tier(Species species, float kg) => IsRare(species)
            ? IsBig(species, kg) ? CatchTier.BigRare : CatchTier.Rare
            : IsBig(species, kg) ? CatchTier.BigCommon : CatchTier.Common;
        // Gold/red styles are ready for future explicitly legendary species. No current
        // fish or large common catch is promoted to those tiers by its sale price.
        public static Color Accent(CatchTier tier) => Palette.Hex(tier switch
        {
            CatchTier.BigCommon => "7AE0A0", CatchTier.Rare => "76BDFA",
            CatchTier.BigRare => "C49BFA", CatchTier.Legendary => "F2C66D",
            CatchTier.Mythical => "FA8A91", _ => "E4ECEB"
        });
        public static string Label(CatchTier tier) => tier switch
        {
            CatchTier.BigCommon => "Common · big", CatchTier.Rare => "Rare",
            CatchTier.BigRare => "Rare · big", CatchTier.Legendary => "Legendary",
            CatchTier.Mythical => "Mythical", _ => "Common"
        };
    }

    // Snapshot only fish successfully inserted into the hold. It cannot award coins,
    // update records or mutate the live fish when reopened later.
    public sealed class StoredCatchItem
    {
        public Species Species { get; }
        public float Kg { get; }
        public int Value { get; }
        public CatchTier Tier { get; }
        public StoredCatchItem(CaughtFish fish, GameState state, float now)
        {
            Species = fish.Species; Kg = fish.Kg; Value = state.ValueOf(fish, now);
            Tier = CatchPresentation.Tier(Species, Kg);
        }
    }
    public sealed class NetCatchReceipt
    {
        public IReadOnlyList<StoredCatchItem> Items { get; }
        public float StoredAt { get; }
        public int Count => Items.Count;
        public float Kg => Items.Sum(f => f.Kg);
        public int Value => Items.Sum(f => f.Value);
        public NetCatchReceipt(IEnumerable<CaughtFish> stored, GameState state, float now)
        {
            Items = System.Array.AsReadOnly(stored.Select(f => new StoredCatchItem(f, state, now)).ToArray());
            StoredAt = now;
        }
    }
}

FILE: Assets/Scripts/Game/CatchRewardGraphic.cs
========================================================================
using UnityEngine;
using UnityEngine.UI;

namespace Catchline.Game
{
    // Native Canvas geometry: soft specimen backlight and a quiet navy enamel frame.
    // Reuses the production canvas/capture/input lifecycle; no extra camera or texture.
    public sealed class CatchRewardGraphic : MaskableGraphic
    {
        public bool Halo { get; private set; }
        Color accent;
        float opacity = 1;
        public void Style(Color value, bool halo, float alpha)
        {
            raycastTarget = false;
            if (value == accent && halo == Halo && Mathf.Approximately(alpha, opacity)) return;
            accent = value; Halo = halo; opacity = alpha; SetVerticesDirty();
        }
        Color Alpha(Color c, float amount = 1) { c.a *= opacity * amount; return c; }
        protected override void OnPopulateMesh(VertexHelper vh)
        {
            vh.Clear(); var r = rectTransform.rect;
            if (Halo) { Glow(vh, r); return; }
            var shadow = r; shadow.y -= 5;
            Rounded(vh, shadow, 18, Alpha(new Color(.02f, .09f, .13f, .25f)), Alpha(new Color(.02f, .09f, .13f, .25f)));
            Rounded(vh, r, 18, Alpha(Color.Lerp(Palette.Hex("496574"), accent, .28f)), Alpha(Palette.Hex("355361")));
            r = new Rect(r.x + 1, r.y + 1, r.width - 2, r.height - 2);
            Rounded(vh, r, 17, Alpha(Palette.Hex("193D50")), Alpha(Palette.Hex("102C3D")));
        }
        static void Rounded(VertexHelper vh, Rect r, float radius, Color top, Color bottom)
        {
            radius = Mathf.Min(radius, Mathf.Min(r.width, r.height) * .5f);
            int first = vh.currentVertCount;
            vh.AddVert(r.center, Color.Lerp(bottom, top, .5f), Vector2.zero);
            for (int corner = 0; corner < 4; corner++)
            {
                float cx = corner is 0 or 3 ? r.xMax - radius : r.xMin + radius;
                float cy = corner < 2 ? r.yMax - radius : r.yMin + radius;
                for (int step = 0; step <= 10; step++)
                {
                    float angle = (corner * 90 + step * 9) * Mathf.Deg2Rad;
                    var p = new Vector2(cx + Mathf.Cos(angle) * radius, cy + Mathf.Sin(angle) * radius);
                    vh.AddVert(p, Color.Lerp(bottom, top, Mathf.InverseLerp(r.yMin, r.yMax, p.y)), Vector2.zero);
                }
            }
            const int count = 44;
            for (int i = 0; i < count; i++) vh.AddTriangle(first, first + 1 + i, first + 1 + (i + 1) % count);
        }
        void Glow(VertexHelper vh, Rect r)
        {
            const int segments = 64, rings = 8;
            var center = r.center;
            vh.AddVert(center, Alpha(accent, .30f), Vector2.zero);
            for (int ring = 1; ring <= rings; ring++)
            {
                float t = ring / (float)rings;
                float alpha = Mathf.Pow(1 - t * t, 3) * .30f;
                for (int i = 0; i < segments; i++)
                {
                    float a = i * Mathf.PI * 2 / segments;
                    vh.AddVert(center + new Vector2(Mathf.Cos(a) * r.width * .5f * t,
                        Mathf.Sin(a) * r.height * .5f * t), Alpha(accent, alpha), Vector2.zero);
                    int index = 1 + (ring - 1) * segments + i;
                    int next = 1 + (ring - 1) * segments + (i + 1) % segments;
                    if (ring == 1) vh.AddTriangle(0, index, next);
                    else
                    {
                        vh.AddTriangle(index - segments, index, next);
                        vh.AddTriangle(index - segments, next, next - segments);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
